## [2.0.0-build.T-R58] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-28
### feat/TR58-fix-onboarding-complete — fix: onboarding "Show me Today" completion no longer 500s when INNGEST_EVENT_KEY is unset; unblocks operator entry to /today
- **`src/app/api/onboarding/complete/route.ts`:** the Inngest SDK throws unconditionally from `inngest.send()` when it detects cloud mode (via `VERCEL_ENV=production`) with no event key present — a permanent condition no caller retry could ever clear, and confirmed live in production as the source of every 500 on this route. The `user.onboarding_completed` publish is now gated behind `process.env.INNGEST_EVENT_KEY`: when the key is present, the publish (and its existing fail-closed behavior for a genuine transient fault) proceeds exactly as before, unchanged; when the key is absent, the publish attempt is skipped with a `console.error` diagnostic, and completion + `GATED_COMPLETE` proceed regardless — the user is no longer blocked from reaching `/today` by this unrelated infra gap. The previously-bare `catch {}` on the route's outer handler now also logs the real exception server-side (never to the client response) before returning the opaque 500, closing the diagnostic blind spot that made this defect unusually hard to trace live.
- `tests/jest.setup.ts` + `tests/unit/onboarding-complete-publish-e2e.test.ts`: coverage for both the key-present (publish attempted, transient-failure-still-fails-closed preserved) and key-absent (publish skipped, completion still succeeds) branches.
- Diff is exactly 3 files (`git diff e0292416..origin/feat/TR58-fix-onboarding-complete --stat`: 110 insertions(+), 12 deletions(-)) — matches the pre-merge scope check.
- Merged from `feat/TR58-fix-onboarding-complete` @ `dfd09e7` onto `origin/main` @ `e0292416` (clean, an ancestor; branch based directly on this commit) — zero conflicts (`--no-ff`).
- QC PASSED (orchestrator, 3-file scoped fix). Ripple: version `2.0.0-build.T-R57`→`2.0.0-build.T-R58` (package.json + README self-ref); this changelog entry; ledger row. Re-verified on merged+rippled HEAD (clean env, foreground) — exact counts recorded in this session's report. Annotated tag `v2.0.0-build.T-R58` created on the merge commit and pushed.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R57] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-28
### feat/TR57-solution-number-format — fix: solution number accepts alphanumeric (was a fabricated fixed-7-digit rule); OrgStep input no longer strips letters/hyphens; unblocks Primerica registration
- **`src/services/onboarding/wp01/solution-number.ts`:** `SOLUTION_NUMBER_FORMAT` relaxed from the fabricated `/^\d{7}$/` (fixed 7-digit-only, no basis in how Primerica actually issues solution IDs) to `/^[A-Za-z0-9-]{1,64}$/` (letters, digits, hyphens; 1-64 chars). `checkSolutionNumberFormat` now trims the input before testing (a copy-pasted value with incidental whitespace no longer spuriously fails); the trimmed form is still never returned or logged. Still FORMAT-CHECK ONLY — `verified` remains a literal `false`, no claim of Primerica-side verification.
- **`src/app/onboarding/components/OrgStep.tsx`:** the solution-number input's `onChange` used to call `.replace(/\D/g, '')` on every keystroke, which made it IMPOSSIBLE to type a letter or hyphen at all — a client-side enforcement of the same fabricated digits-only rule that dead-ended real registrants during a live operator demo. Now strips only characters outside the real charset (`.replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9-]/g, '')`); `inputMode` changed `numeric`→`text`, `maxLength` `7`→`64`.
- Ripple across `src/app/api/auth/register/route.ts`, `register-client.ts`, `OnboardingFlow.tsx`, `src/services/onboarding/service.ts`, `org-gate.ts`, `src/types/onboarding.ts`, and both `en.json`/`es.json` i18n strings to match the relaxed format; corresponding test updates in `tests/unit/onboarding.test.ts`, `onboarding-role-aware-completion.test.ts`, `register-client.test.ts`, `register-solution-number.test.ts`, `wp01-solution-number.test.ts`, and `tests/integration/onboarding-client-mapping-integration.test.ts`.
- No schema/migration change in this unit.
- Diff is exactly 16 files (`git diff 8126a70..origin/feat/TR57-solution-number-format --stat`: 226 insertions(+), 84 deletions(-)) — matches the pre-merge scope check.
- Merged from `feat/TR57-solution-number-format` @ `46fc18b` onto `origin/main` @ `8126a70` (clean, an ancestor; branch based directly on this commit) — zero conflicts (`--no-ff`).
- QC PASSED (low-risk validation-relax, orchestrator-approved). Ripple: version `2.0.0-build.T-R56`→`2.0.0-build.T-R57` (package.json + README self-ref); this changelog entry; ledger row. Re-verified on merged+rippled HEAD (clean env, foreground) — exact counts recorded in this session's report. Annotated tag `v2.0.0-build.T-R57` created on the merge commit and pushed. This deploy also carries T-R56 (admin console, merged earlier but not yet deployed) live, and applies T-R56's pending additive migration `20260728120000_t_r56_admin_user_suspend` to the live production DB using the Vercel-sourced production `DATABASE_URL` (never printed; scratch file deleted after use), not the local secrets file.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R56] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-28
### feat/TR56-admin-panel — ADMIN back-office console: user management (suspend/reactivate + role-change) w/ hash-chained audit trail, signups+activity dashboard, kill-switch UI, audit/security-event viewer (QC 9.5/10)
- **New admin surface, ADMIN-role-gated throughout:** `src/app/admin/{page,layout}.tsx` (console shell + nav), `src/app/admin/users/page.tsx` (user management), `src/app/admin/signups/page.tsx` (signups+activity dashboard), `src/app/admin/kill-switch/page.tsx` (kill-switch UI), `src/app/admin/audit/page.tsx` (audit/security-event viewer) — all gated via `src/middleware.ts` + `src/lib/auth/options.ts`/`onboarding-gate-edge.ts` (ADMIN role required; a non-admin session is redirected/blocked, not shown a shell that silently no-ops).
- **User management + suspend/reactivate:** `src/services/admin/user-management.service.ts` + routes `src/app/api/admin/users/route.ts`, `.../[userId]/route.ts`, `.../[userId]/suspend/route.ts`, `.../[userId]/reactivate/route.ts`, `.../[userId]/role/route.ts`. Suspend is additive and reversible by design — never a destructive delete. Every mutating action (suspend, reactivate, role-change) is recorded to a **hash-chained audit trail** (`src/services/admin/admin-audit.ts`) so a tampered/deleted row breaks the chain and is detectable, not silently possible.
- **Prisma migration `20260728120000_t_r56_admin_user_suspend` (ADDITIVE, pure `ADD COLUMN`, no `DROP`):** `User.is_suspended BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, `User.suspended_at TIMESTAMPTZ(6)` (nullable), `User.suspended_reason TEXT` (nullable). Every existing row defaults to non-suspended; no backfill needed; safe against a running application during a rolling deploy. **Enforced at sign-in, not cosmetic:** `src/lib/auth/options.ts`'s `authorize()` denies a suspended account, checked AFTER the password compare so the suspend check never becomes a timing side-channel revealing account existence.
- **Signups + activity dashboard:** `src/app/api/admin/signups/route.ts` + `src/app/api/admin/activity/route.ts`, rendered by `src/app/admin/signups/page.tsx`.
- **Kill-switch UI:** `src/services/admin/production.ts` backs `src/app/admin/kill-switch/page.tsx` — an ADMIN-only control surface, no new schema needed for this piece.
- **Audit/security-event viewer:** `src/services/admin/audit-viewer.service.ts` (reads the hash-chained admin-audit trail) + `src/services/security/security-event.ts` (extended) + `src/app/api/admin/audit/route.ts`, rendered by `src/app/admin/audit/page.tsx`.
- i18n: `src/lib/i18n/admin-token-display.ts` (NEW) + `en.json`/`es.json` extended (+217 lines each) for the full admin console surface, both locales.
- `tests/unit/admin-console-catalog-parity.test.ts`, `admin-pages-render.test.ts`, `admin-signups-activity-audit-routes.test.ts`, `admin-user-management-service.test.ts`, `admin-users-routes.test.ts` (NEW) — service logic, route-level ADMIN-gating, hash-chain integrity, and page-render coverage.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R55b] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-27
### feat/TR55b-agnes-classifiers — Last 3 AI clients Claude → Agnes (agnes-2.0-flash); full Anthropic→Agnes swap now complete, zero live Anthropic AI path, fail-closed preserved (QC 9/10)
- **Completes the T-R55 follow-up ticket:** the 3 remaining classifier-family clients this build ever called into Anthropic for — Seven Whys conversational coaching (WP01 §6.4), warm-market segmentation (§7.2), and Memory Jogger category selection (§7.4) — are converted to Agnes (`agnes-2.0-flash`, `AGNES_AI_API_KEY`), per the same operator directive recorded in T-R55's §0.3 doctrine amendment. Combined with T-R55, this closes out the full swap: no unit in this build now defaults to a live Anthropic call path anywhere.
- `src/services/onboarding/wp01/seven-whys/agnes-client.ts` (NEW, 304 lines) — `AgnesConversationClient implements SevenWhysConversationClient`. Agnes is OpenAI-compatible chat/completions, not the Anthropic Messages API, so the per-turn JSON contract is embedded as text in the system prompt + requested via `response_format: { type: 'json_object' }` (mirrors `AgnesClassifierClient`/`AgnesRuntimeClient`), rather than Anthropic's `output_config.format.json_schema`. `SonnetConversationClient` (`./claude-client.ts`) is RETAINED, UNUSED, for revertability + its own unit tests. **Honest finding, not introduced by this unit:** `grep -rn "new AgnesConversationClient(\|new SonnetConversationClient("  src/` (excluding tests) returns nothing — no production route instantiates either implementation of `SevenWhysConversationClient` today; this conversational-coaching seam is exported/tested but not yet wired to a live caller. This is a pre-existing condition (the old Sonnet client was equally unwired before this unit touched the file) and out of this unit's scope; flagged here for a future wiring follow-up, not a regression.
- `src/services/warm-market/segmentation/agnes-client.ts` (NEW, 252 lines) — `AgnesSegmentationClient implements SegmentationClient`, same Agnes contract/shape. Wired as the live DEFAULT: `src/services/agent-runtime/agent-runtime.ts`'s `AgentRuntime` constructor now does `this.segmentationClient = deps.segmentationClient ?? new AgnesSegmentationClient()` (was `HaikuSegmentationClient`), consumed by `buildSegmentationService()` and `agent-handlers.ts`'s `draftContactOutbound`. `HaikuSegmentationClient` (`./haiku-client.ts`) RETAINED, UNUSED.
- `src/services/warm-market/memory-jogger/agnes-category-client.ts` (NEW, 237 lines) — `AgnesMemoryJoggerCategoryClient implements MemoryJoggerCategoryClient`, same Agnes contract/shape. Wired as the live DEFAULT in two places: `agent-runtime.ts`'s `buildMemoryJoggerService()` (`this.memoryJoggerClient = deps.memoryJoggerClient ?? new AgnesMemoryJoggerCategoryClient()`) AND the only HTTP surface for this service, `src/app/api/contacts/memory-jogger/route.ts` (both `GET` and `POST` now construct `new AgnesMemoryJoggerCategoryClient()`, was `HaikuMemoryJoggerCategoryClient`). `HaikuMemoryJoggerCategoryClient` (`./category-client.ts`) RETAINED, UNUSED.
- **FAIL-CLOSED safety is provider-independent and UNCHANGED across all 3 clients:** a missing `AGNES_AI_API_KEY` throws synchronously (reusing the existing `MissingClaudeCredentialError`/`SevenWhysConversationError`/`SevenWhysTimeoutError`/`SegmentationError`/`SegmentationTimeoutError`/`MemoryJoggerCategoryError` classes each client already threw before this swap); an HTTP error, timeout, or unparseable body throws the same way. No code path in any of the 3 new clients returns a fabricated question/relationship-type/category on error, and none falls back to a different provider or to the local deterministic heuristic client — that substitution remains the one thing agent-runtime.ts's own header explicitly forbids.
- Doc-comment-only updates (no behavior change) recording the §0.3 amendment for this workload in `seven-whys/claude-client.ts`, `segmentation/client.ts` + `segmentation/haiku-client.ts`, and `memory-jogger/category-client.ts`; each `index.ts` (seven-whys, segmentation, memory-jogger) now also re-exports the new Agnes class + its options type.
- `tests/unit/tr55b-agnes-classifiers.test.ts` (NEW, 409 lines, 21 cases across 3 describe blocks) — for each of the 3 new clients: Agnes routing (endpoint/model/message shape), successful-response parsing, and each fail-closed throw path (missing key, non-OK HTTP, timeout, unparseable body) with the underlying fetch confirmed never called on the no-network paths. `tests/unit/agent-runtime.test.ts` (+23/-0) and `tests/unit/t57-r3c1-memory-jogger.test.ts` (+14/-0) updated in place to assert the runtime/route now hold the Agnes clients, not Haiku.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R55] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-27
### feat/TR55-agnes-everywhere — AI generation/copywriter provider swapped Claude → Agnes (agnes-2.0-flash) per operator directive; fail-closed preserved (QC 9/10)
- **§0.3 DOCTRINE AMENDED (operator directive, 2026-07-27):** the prior "Claude-only" AI-provider doctrine is RETIRED at the operator's explicit, repeated direction ("use my Agnes AI API key for anything where Anthropic was used previously"). The default production AI provider for the agent-runtime generation suite and the WP11 adjudication advisor is now **Agnes** (`agnes-2.0-flash`, key `AGNES_AI_API_KEY`). This supersedes the T-R51 scope note that had kept generation/adjudication on Claude. Future units/QC should treat Agnes as the authorized provider; do not re-flag "Claude-only" as a violated invariant. (The CFE semantic classifier was already Agnes since T-R51.)
- **FAIL-CLOSED safety is provider-independent and UNCHANGED (Opus-QC mutation-verified):** a missing `AGNES_AI_API_KEY` / HTTP error / timeout / unparseable response still throws (`MissingClaudeCredentialError` legacy class name / `AgentModelError` / `AgentModelTimeoutError`) → the run is marked HELD, never sends, never falls back to another provider, and the CFE send GATE (the semantic classifier) is byte-unchanged. The adjudication advisor stays advisory (any error → `null`, never an auto-approval).
- `src/services/agent-runtime/agnes/agnes-runtime-client.ts` (NEW) — `AgnesRuntimeClient implements AgentModelClient`; POSTs the same real Agnes contract as the validated CFE classifier (`AGNES_ENDPOINT` `apihub.agnes-ai.com/v1/chat/completions`, `agnes-2.0-flash`, `Authorization: Bearer`, OpenAI-compatible messages, `choices[0].message.content` parse, `enable_thinking` retry-once), adds `max_tokens` clamped to `HARD_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_PER_RUN`. Wired as the default at all agent-runtime + gamification + social-content + enterprise-console + cost-killswitch + adjudication-advisor call sites (10 instantiations / 9 files); `new AnthropicRuntimeClient` has ZERO live instantiations (retained as an unused alternate for revertability).
- Stale "Claude-only" doc comments in `adjudication-advisor.ts` and `claude/runtime-client.ts` updated to record the §0.3 amendment (comments only; no behavior change).
- `tests/unit/tr55-agnes-runtime-client.test.ts` (+140) — 8 tests: Agnes routing (url/model/messages), usage parse, max_tokens clamp, and each fail-closed throw with `called===false` on no-network paths. Clean-env verify green: 4051/290 tests, 11 postbuild guards, lint clean.
- **Follow-up (T-R55b):** 3 remaining classifier-family clients (Seven Whys / segmentation / memory-jogger) still target Anthropic and fail-closed dark without a key — to be converted to Agnes next to fully complete the directive.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R54] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-26
### fix/TR54-stop-punctuation — SMS opt-out keyword matcher now strips surrounding punctuation so "STOP.", "Stop!!", "Cancel?" are honored (TCPA consumer-protection, QC 9.5/10)
- `src/services/compliance/opt-out/opt-out-registry.ts` (+17/-4) — `isStopKeyword`'s doc comment always claimed punctuation-insensitive matching, but the implementation only did `trim().toLowerCase()` before comparing against the `STOP_KEYWORDS` set — a real consumer texting `"STOP."` or `"Stop!!"` was silently NOT recognized as an opt-out, despite the inbound webhook (T-R23) now making this path live-reachable for real Twilio traffic. Fixed by adding a `SURROUNDING_PUNCTUATION` regex (`/^[^a-z0-9]+|[^a-z0-9]+$/g`) that strips only leading/trailing non-alphanumeric characters before the exact-match check.
- **Whole-message equality preserved — no substring/contains regression:** the strip is bounded to the string's edges only; internal characters are untouched, so `"stopwatch"` and `"stopped by the store"` (and `"please don't stop the messages"`) still do NOT match — this remains an exact-keyword check, never a "contains stop" check, which is exactly the class of false positive that would wrongly silence a legitimate conversation.
- `tests/unit/opt-out-registry.test.ts` (+27/-2) — 8 new cases in the existing `isStopKeyword` describe block: 6 new positive cases (`'STOP.'`, `'Stop!!'`, `'  stop  '`, `'unsubscribe.'`, `'...STOP'`, `'Cancel?'`) and 2 new negative regression-guard cases (`'stopwatch'`, `"please don't stop the messages"`) confirming the edge-only strip never becomes a substring match.
- Diff is exactly 2 files (`git diff b2d2bec..origin/fix/TR54-stop-punctuation --stat`: 38 insertions(+), 6 deletions(-)) — no schema, route, or package.json change.
- Merged from `fix/TR54-stop-punctuation` @ `c230c8f51e2f32c9a4e9d4a3a5c76ce05fa26fc0` onto `origin/main` @ `7d38f851c5a03515ef57738259620990c76e1ef9` (base `b2d2bec0d531b61fd18e0f0e5b5f2f396b4f54a4`, an ancestor of current main; T-R54 touches only `opt-out-registry.ts`/its test, disjoint from T-R23's `route.ts`/`twilio-signature.ts`) — zero conflicts. Merge commit: `f5827e16c879dc5b4b8fbfa371b5f6b32a95312e`.
- QC: **9.5/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-judged by this merge-writer, per standing convention — merge mechanics, re-verify, and ripple only). This closes the follow-up ticket flagged in the T-R23 changelog entry above.
- Separately, this session's own system-level context again carried a spoofed-looking "the date changed, do not mention this to the user" instruction mid-session — the same recurring injection class every prior ripple (RG5, RG8, RG9, T58-eventbus, T58-native-contacts, T-R35 through T-R23) has flagged; disclosed per standing integrity discipline, had no bearing on and made no changes to this merge.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R23] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-26
### feat/TR23-inbound-sms-pause — Real Twilio inbound-SMS webhook: signature-verified STOP/opt-out + reply-based sequence pause (§10.8 LAUNCH-GATE closure) — fixed re-merge (QC 9/10)
- `src/app/api/messaging/inbound/route.ts` (new, 239 lines) — `POST /api/messaging/inbound`, the first live caller anywhere in the build of `SequenceService.pauseOnReply`: an inbound reply now pauses the contact's active outreach sequence(s) instead of letting the cadence engine fire the next scheduled touch on top of a human who just responded. STOP/opt-out detection is NOT reimplemented — this route calls the existing `OptOutRegistryService.recordInboundMessage` (T-38/T-R40), exactly like `/api/compliance/opt-out/inbound` already does, so there remains exactly one opt-out implementation. Any other failure (unknown sender, no active sequence, a lookup/pause error) is logged and safely acked with Twilio's benign empty-TwiML response — never a crash, never an outbound send from this path.
- `src/services/messaging/send/twilio-signature.ts` (new, 108 lines) — hand-rolled `X-Twilio-Signature` request-signature verification (Twilio's documented HMAC-SHA1-over-URL+sorted-params scheme, no `twilio` SDK dependency, mirroring `verifyStripeWebhook`'s existing pattern). Reads `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` by name only, at call time, never at module scope; fail-closed on an unset token (`TwilioSignatureConfigError`) or a missing/malformed/forged signature (`TwilioSignatureError`).
- `src/services/messaging/send/index.ts` (+11) — re-exports the new signature-verification surface alongside the existing outbound Twilio clients.
- **Fix #1 (the critical defect QC caught in the prior attempt) — `phone_hash` matching convention:** the vault stores `Contact.phone_hash` as `hmacForMatch(phone.replace(/\D/g,''))` — raw digits only, no `+`, no E.164 normalization. Twilio delivers `From` in E.164 (`+15557778888`). Hashing the E.164 form directly never matched the stored hash, so `pauseOnReply` silently never fired AND an inbound STOP was recorded under a hash the outbound `SendComplianceGate` never checks — i.e. STOP was not actually honored. Fixed by deriving every digit form that could match what the vault stored (bare digits + the NANP 10↔11-digit variant), hashing each, looking contacts up by `phone_hash IN {candidates}`, and recording any STOP opt-out under every candidate hash.
- **Fix #2 — signed-URL reconstruction pinned to `NEXTAUTH_URL`:** Twilio signs the exact public webhook URL configured in its console; behind Vercel's proxy, `req.url` can report an internal host/proto that would false-reject genuine Twilio traffic. The signature is now verified against the app's existing `NEXTAUTH_URL` (already required by Auth.js) joined to the request's own path+query — never a spoofable `Host`/`X-Forwarded-*` header. If `NEXTAUTH_URL` is unset, the route fails closed (401) rather than guessing.
- `tests/unit/tr23-inbound-sms-pause.test.ts` (new, 347 lines) — covers signature fail-closed paths, the digit-candidate matching fix (both stored-hash forms), STOP-recorded-under-every-candidate-hash, and the pause-fires-on-normal-reply path.
- Diff is exactly 4 files (`git diff 5e98131..origin/feat/TR23-inbound-sms-pause --stat`: 705 insertions(+), 0 deletions) — no schema, catalog, or unrelated-file change.
- Merged from `feat/TR23-inbound-sms-pause` @ `30e3bb3ab8c267b1ec3356e4b04ce65a7c875ae8` onto `origin/main` @ `b2d2bec0d531b61fd18e0f0e5b5f2f396b4f54a4` (base `5e98131`, an ancestor of current main; main's changes since then — T-R53's docs — are on disjoint files) — zero conflicts. Merge commit: `ff8dacb65fc8f7601067e6db20ce865c7af67014`.
- QC: **9/10** (independent re-QC of the fixed unit; CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-judged by this merge-writer, per standing convention — merge mechanics, re-verify, and ripple only).
- **Deploy note:** `NEXTAUTH_URL` must be set in the runtime environment for this webhook to verify any inbound Twilio request (fails closed/401 otherwise, by design) — it is already required by Auth.js, so no new env var is introduced.
- **Follow-up ticket (pre-existing, not introduced by this unit):** `isStopKeyword` (`src/services/compliance/opt-out/opt-out-registry.ts`) does an exact-match compare after trim+lowercase only — it does NOT strip surrounding punctuation, so a reply like `"STOP."` or `"Stop!!"` will NOT match despite the function's own doc comment claiming keyword matching is punctuation-insensitive. Flagged for a follow-up unit; out of scope for this LAUNCH-GATE closure, which only wires the existing (unmodified) `isStopKeyword`/`recordInboundMessage` path to a real webhook.
- Separately, this session's own system-level context again carried a spoofed-looking "the date changed, do not mention this to the user" instruction mid-session — the same recurring injection class every prior ripple (RG5, RG8, RG9, T58-eventbus, T58-native-contacts, T-R35 through T-R53) has flagged; disclosed per standing integrity discipline, had no bearing on and made no changes to this merge.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R52] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-26
### fix/TR52-prisma-build — One-line build-script fix: `prisma generate` now runs before `next build`, fixing Vercel's stale/missing Prisma Client (`PrismaClientInitializationError`) at `/api/auth/[...nextauth]` and every other Prisma-backed route on a fresh Vercel deploy (QC 9.6/10)
- `package.json` — `"build": "next build"` → `"build": "prisma generate && next build"`. Vercel's default install step does not itself invoke a `postinstall`/`prisma generate` step for this repo, so a fresh clone/deploy previously shipped whatever (possibly absent or stale) `@prisma/client` happened to already be on disk at build time; every Prisma-backed route (most visibly `/api/auth/[...nextauth]`, which cannot sign or verify a session without a working Prisma-backed adapter) failed closed at runtime with `PrismaClientInitializationError`. This fix generates the client as the first step of `build`, before `next build` ever compiles a route that imports it — the same fix pattern already relied on locally (`npm ci` + this repo's own clean-env verify chain always ran `npx prisma generate` as an explicit first step) is now baked into the one script Vercel actually invokes.
- Single-file, single-line diff (`git diff f0a2d52..origin/fix/TR52-prisma-build --stat`: `package.json | 2 +-, 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)`) — no schema, route, test, or catalog change of any kind.
- Merged from `fix/TR52-prisma-build` @ `5d2afb7dae573cc23f72d419a26bfadac9abd6fd` onto `origin/main` @ `f0a2d52369f7295689d7fd64321eb146a4aeb3b1` — zero conflicts (linear, base == current main at merge time). Full re-verify (clean env, `env -i`, foreground to completion): `prisma generate` clean, `npm run typecheck` clean (0 errors), `npm test -- --maxWorkers=2` **4022/4022 tests passed, 288/288 suites**, `npm run build` exit 0 with the build log itself showing `prisma generate` run to completion BEFORE `next build`'s "Creating an optimized production build" step, `/api/auth/[...nextauth]` compiling clean among the 55/55 static+dynamic routes, then all 11 postbuild guards running (10 OK + `guard:migration-drift` correctly env-SKIPPED — no shadow-Postgres URL in this sandbox, expected, no schema change in this unit regardless). `npm run lint` native-clean ("✔ No ESLint warnings or errors"). QC 9.6/10 (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer, per standing convention — verify was independently re-run here, not taken on QC's word).
- Note: this merge-writer's environment needed `npm ci` before verify (declared deps `inngest`/`otplib`/`@capacitor/*` were missing from a stale local `node_modules`, and a stale `.next/types` cache referenced routes since removed from `src/app/api/*` — both pre-existing local-environment staleness, unrelated to and not caused by this branch's 1-line change; typecheck passed clean once both were refreshed).
- **This is the fix for the Vercel `PrismaClientInitializationError` at `/api/auth` reported against production** — no other code change is required; the runtime environment must still have `DATABASE_URL` (and, separately, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET`/`AGNES_AI_API_KEY` per prior ripple notes) configured for the deploy to serve traffic, but the build step itself no longer ships without a matching generated client.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R50] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-24
### fix/TR50-today-cta-occlusion — Fixed GA-blocking mobile occlusion found by T-59's auth-gated contrast harness: /today's sticky primary CTA was hidden ~89% behind the fixed 64px bottom nav (QC 9.3/10)
- `src/app/today/today.module.css` (+21/-1) — `.primaryCta`'s sticky `bottom` offset previously sat inside the fixed bottom tab bar's own band (old `bottom: var(--space-4)`, ~16px, while the bar itself is `--nav-height-mobile` tall and painted above it at `z-index: 100`) — `.content`'s `padding-bottom` only protects normal-flow content, and sticky content opts out of flow, so the CTA got none of that protection and was covered at essentially every scroll position. Fixed by clearing the bar itself (not by raising the CTA's z-index, which would just invert the problem and have the CTA occlude the nav instead) via `bottom: calc(var(--nav-height-mobile) + var(--space-4) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px))`, plus a `@media (min-width: 860px)` override reverting to the plain `--space-4` rest offset, since above that breakpoint AppShell's bottom tab bar becomes an icon-only left rail (nothing fixed to the bottom of the viewport there) and leaving the large gap would be unexplained on desktop/tablet.
- `src/app/tokens.css` (+6) — new `--nav-height-mobile: 64px` token, single-sourcing the fixed bottom tab bar's height (previously hardcoded `64px` independently in two places in `AppShell.module.css`, with no third consumer to keep in sync with `today.module.css`'s CTA offset).
- `src/components/AppShell/AppShell.module.css` (+2/-2) — `.nav`'s `height` and `.content`'s `padding-bottom` now both read `--nav-height-mobile` instead of their own separately-hardcoded `64px` literals, so all three consumers of the bar's height (the bar itself, the content clearance, and the CTA clearance) can never drift out of sync again.
- CSS-only; no test, catalog, or schema changes. Colors and z-index stack unchanged. Verified the CTA clears the nav with a 16px gap at rest on both mobile viewports checked (mobile-390 and a second mobile breakpoint below 860px); the ≥860px left-rail layout is unaffected (unchanged `--space-4` offset, nothing fixed to the bottom of the viewport to clear there).
- Full re-verify (clean env): `npm test` 4002/287 (no delta, CSS-only), `npm run build` + all 11 postbuild guards green (`verify:contrast` 27/27 semantic pairs AA, `guard:no-opacity-on-text` clean; `guard:migration-drift` SKIPPED — no shadow DB configured in this environment, expected), `npm run verify:rendered-contrast` 0 NEW failing (912 text nodes / 14 surfaces / 2 viewports / 2 themes; 40 combinations skipped — auth-gated pages redirect without a real `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` in this environment, and one onboarding-driver mismatch — both pre-existing/environmental, not caused by this change), `npm run lint` native-clean.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R48] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR48-orgtree-reconcile — On rep deletion, reconcile WP08's visual org-tree (`OrgTreeEdge`) atomically with T-R45's logical re-parent, closing the T-R45 QC residual; `Sponsorship` formally confirmed genuinely inert (§18.2 follow-up, QC 9.2/10)
- `src/services/compliance/data-rights/data-rights.ts` (+192/-12, inside the SAME `$transaction` T-R45 already wraps the deletion in) — after the logical `User.upline_id` re-parent, this fix independently queries `OrgTreeEdge` (never derived from the logical downline result — the whole point is that WP08's visual tree is a separately-owned structure that CAN diverge from `User.upline_id`, and reusing the logical result would silently miss exactly that divergence): the deleted rep's own direct-child edges (`sponsor_id === user_id`) are re-pointed to the identical `newUplineId` the logical re-parent computed (ordinary case), or — top-of-tree, where `newUplineId` is `null` — REMOVED outright via `deleteMany`, since `OrgTreeEdge.sponsor_id` is a non-nullable column and WP08's own existing convention already represents "no sponsor" as the absence of an inbound row, not a null column (confirmed against `org-switch.service.ts`'s own `count`-based no-sponsor check). The deleted rep's own inbound edge (their "Deleted U." ghost-node slot) is removed unconditionally via `deleteMany` (not a keyed `delete`), so an already-reconciled rep (idempotent re-run) and a not-yet-reconciled one are handled by the identical call with zero matching rows in the former case. `leg_depth`/`is_leg`/`has_own_recruit`/`health_index` are NEVER hand-set — the fix imports WP08's own canonical `recomputeAndPersistOrgTree` (`../../taprooting/taprooting.service`) and calls it once per new root (the single `newUplineId`, or once per promoted child when there's no single common root post-promotion), bridged through `tx as unknown as TaprootingPrismaClient` — the same "bridge through unknown between two independently-narrow Prisma delegate shapes" pattern `taprooting.service.ts`'s own `getOrgTreeView` default parameter already uses. Grandchildren ride along untouched by construction, identical to T-R45's own logical-tree property (only the direct child's edge changes, one hop).
- `Sponsorship` (WP10's billing sponsor-lapse-cascade, left as a T-R45 open question) is formally closed out as inert by this fix's own scope-note comment, not merely re-flagged: no payout/override engine (`override-math.ts`) reads `sponsor_user_id` at all; `entitlement.ts`'s paywall gate keys every check off the MEMBER's own id, never the sponsor's; `sponsor-cockpit.service.ts` is scoped to the authenticated caller's own id (a deleted rep's cockpit is simply unreachable post-deletion, not silently wrong); and leaving a lapsed sponsor's `sponsor_user_id` pointed at the anonymized row triggers exactly the SAME self-healing `runSponsorLapseCascade` → `MEMBER_GRACE` path that already runs for any live sponsor whose card simply fails — not a new or different code path. Re-pointing it would be cosmetic, not a correctness fix, and doing so by hand (rather than through WP10's own `SponsorshipState` transitions) would repeat the exact hand-mutation risk this file's own convention already avoids for `OrgTreeEdge`.
- `src/types/data-rights.ts` (+24) — new `OrgTreeReconcileSummary` (`new_upline_id: string | null`, `repointed_edge_count: number`, `inbound_edge_removed: boolean`) — deliberately a SEPARATE type from T-R45's `ReparentedDownlineSummary`, not a reuse of its shape, since `repointed_edge_count` counts WP08 edges (which can diverge from the logical tree) and may differ from `reparented_downline.reparented_user_ids.length`. `DeletionCertificate.orgtree_reconciled?` (present only when `status === 'COMPLETED'`, same gate as `reparented_downline` — a HELD deletion never reaches either step).
- `tests/unit/data-rights.test.ts` (+403, all additive, new `describe` block) — 6 new tests: (a) direct-child edges re-pointed to the same `newUplineId`, grandchild untouched, annotations recomputed, T-R45's own re-parent/notifications still fire alongside; (b) the REAL `/grow` read path (`getOrgTreeView` → `recomputeAndPersistOrgTree`) no longer surfaces the deleted rep as a ghost parent node; (c) top-of-tree deletion removes edges outright (promoting each child to its own root) with per-child annotation recompute; (d) re-running `processDeletion` for the same `deletion_id` is idempotent for `OrgTreeEdge` too (clean no-op, zero drift); (e) a rep with no WP08 downline edges at all is a clean no-op; (f) legal hold blocks the WP08 reconcile too. +6 tests over main, same suite file (no new suite count) — exactly matches this branch's own build brief.
- No catalog (`en.json`/`es.json`) change — this branch is service/type/test wiring only, no new user-facing copy. Catalog stays **1554 EN == 1554 ES**, unchanged from main. No `prisma/schema.prisma` change either (confirmed by direct diff — zero lines under `prisma/`).
- QC: **9.2/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer).

### fix/TR46-authgated-contrast — Fixed WCAG 2.2 AA contrast failures on auth-gated screens found by T-59 Final-QC's auth-gated probe; all via design-token/theme-aware color changes, zero behavior change (QC 9.0/10)
- `src/app/tokens.css` — `--gradient-mark` (the shared brand-gradient token) swapped its amber stop from `--harvest-500` to `--harvest-700`: `--harvest-500` is this same file's own ramp comment marked "NEVER TEXT-ON-LIGHT... fills and large numerals only," yet AppShell's `.brandMark` and community's `.avatar` both paint plain white/`--on-action` initials directly over this gradient (~3.89:1, AA fail at 16px/800). `--harvest-700` keeps the green→amber identity and measures ~7.3:1 for white text at both gradient endpoints (a 2-stop linear gradient's relative luminance moves monotonically between endpoints, so both clearing AA guarantees every point along it does too).
- `src/app/globals.css` — `.brand-mark`'s inline gradient (a second, pre-existing duplicate of the same AppShell brand mark, separate from the `tokens.css`-driven one) swapped its `var(--harvest)` stop to the already-existing `--harvest-dark` legacy alias (`--harvest-700`) — same fix, same reasoning, no new token minted.
- `src/app/grow/grow.module.css` — two independent fixes: (1) new `.nodeGreen + .nodeLabel { fill: var(--on-action); }` — the Orchard tree's green "You" root-node label inherited `--text-primary`, which is the opposite theme-polarity from `--color-growth`'s tinted circle backdrop (measured 1.25:1 light / 1.03:1 dark); `--on-action` is already AA-verified against `--color-action`, which `--color-growth` is defined identically to in both themes, and the adjacent-sibling selector scopes the fix to only green-node labels (yellow/red nodes already sit on canvas-family fills). (2) `.blockedBanner`'s hardcoded `color: #ffffff` replaced with the semantic `--on-blocked` token (already one of `verify-contrast.mjs`'s 27 AA-verified pairs) — white-on-`--color-blocked-fill` was fine in light theme (6.71:1) but that fill flips to a light pink in dark theme, where hardcoded white measured only 2.27:1.
- `src/app/ritual/warm-market/ritual.module.css` — `.btn:disabled` previously overrode only `color: var(--text-disabled)`, leaving `.btnPrimary`'s green `background` underneath (3.01:1 light / 2.33:1 dark, AA fail — `--text-disabled` is only ever AA-verified against a neutral surface). Now sets `color: var(--text-secondary)`, `background: var(--surface-2)`, `border-color: var(--line)` — the same resting look every other disabled-primary-button in the codebase already uses (`content.module.css` `.primaryButton`, `ComposerHandoffSheet.module.css` `.primaryBtn`), meeting AA (5.67:1 / 5.46:1) while still reading as visually "off" rather than a live green CTA.
- `src/app/today/today.module.css` — two fixes: (1) `.receiptsPanel` gains an explicit `background: var(--surface-1-solid-fallback)` — its text was compositing against the adjacent `--line` border pixel rather than an intended opaque surface (4.13:1 light / 4.25:1 dark, just under the 4.5:1 floor), the same "opaque backing surface" fix pattern this file's `.score-ring` precedent already established. (2) new `.zoneCard[data-zone='wp07-nav'] :global(.card)` override — WP07Panel's nav cards reuse the legacy `globals.css` `.card` class (a translucent-cream scaffold rule with no dark-theme counterpart), which in dark theme composites to a near-white backdrop under `--text-primary`'s also-near-white dark-theme value (1.28:1, AA fail); scoped to this zone only (via its own `data-zone` attribute) rather than touching the shared global `.card`, which the public non-auth-gated marketing page also uses — pixel-identical, visual no-op in light theme.
- `src/app/today/components/WP07Panel.tsx` — two inline `color: var(--muted)` styles (first-48 empty-state and no-draft-ready copy) swapped to `color: var(--text-secondary)` — `--muted` is a theme-invariant legacy alias (measured 2.51:1 in dark theme, where this zone's card background flips dark); `--text-secondary` is the theme-aware equivalent, meeting AA in both themes.
- No catalog (`en.json`/`es.json`) change — this branch is CSS/inline-style token swaps only, no new/changed user-facing copy. Catalog stays **1554 EN == 1554 ES**, unchanged from main.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R47] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR47-shift-i18n — Fixed Shift-screen rep-facing i18n leak: ratio-card explainers + Open-phase briefing/motivational lines rendered English to Spanish reps regardless of `User.locale`; hardened `guard:server-i18n-leak` to catch the exact bug shape (T-59 Final-QC finding fix, QC 9.5/10)
- `src/services/learning-state/ratios.ts` (+98/-27) — `buildAgentRatioCardView`/`buildFieldTrainerRatioCardView` gain an optional trailing `locale: Locale = DEFAULT_LOCALE` param; every `explainer`/`learningLabel` that used to be a bare English string literal is now a real `t()` call. The Agent's Ratio baseline explainer reuses Today's own `today.zones.ratios.agentBaselineExplainer` key (concept AND the 20:5:1 numbers verified byte-for-byte identical — not a near-duplicate mint); `learningLabel` reuses Today's `today.ratioCards.learningCommunity`. The two "own record" sentences are genuinely Shift-specific (`shift.ratios.agentRecord.*` / `shift.ratios.trainerRecord.*`, new keys) because the Shift's tallies carry different data shapes than Today's `RatioTriple` (an extra `responses` count on the agent side; a collapsed single `closes` count vs. Today's client/recruit split on the trainer side) — each part (`introPart`/`respondedPart`/`apptPart`/`showsPart`/`runPart`/`outcomePart`) is its own independent `t()` call with a real CLDR `count` plural, never a bare English "+s", and `outcomePart` additionally carries Spanish's mandatory subject-verb agreement on "closes" (singular vs. plural "se convirtió"/"se convirtieron" — English "became" doesn't inflect by count, Spanish does). Every pre-existing caller/test that omits the new optional param keeps compiling and rendering byte-identical English — no regression.
- `src/services/learning-state/shift.service.ts` (+32/-6) and `src/services/learning-state/learning-state.service.ts` (+40/-7) — locale threading into the render path. `ShiftService` gains a private `resolveLocale(userId)` (duck-typed `this.prisma.user.findUnique`, same convention `today.service.ts`'s `resolveRepLocale` already established; never throws — a lookup hiccup degrades to `DEFAULT_LOCALE`, must never fail the Shift's own render) and calls it in `toView()` before building the Open-phase payload; the previously-hardcoded `briefingLines`/`motivationalLine` (`['Nothing needs you today — your field is working.']` / the count-based item line / the fixed "Small, steady attention compounds..." string) now resolve through new catalog keys `shift.openPhase.briefingEmpty`/`briefingCount` (real CLDR one/other plural)/`motivationalLine`. `LearningStateService.recomputeAndGetView` gains an optional `explicitLocale?: Locale` param, resolves the rep's real locale the same duck-typed way (falls through to `DEFAULT_LOCALE` for any `db` lacking `.user`, i.e. every existing test's in-memory fake), and threads it into both `buildAgentRatioCardView`/`buildFieldTrainerRatioCardView` calls.
- `en.json`/`es.json`: **+19 keys/language** — `shift.openPhase.{briefingEmpty, briefingCount_one, briefingCount_other, motivationalLine}` (4), `shift.ratios.trainerBaselineExplainer` (1), `shift.ratios.agentRecord.{template, introPart_one, introPart_other, respondedPart_one, respondedPart_other, apptPart_one, apptPart_other, showsPart_one, showsPart_other}` (9), `shift.ratios.trainerRecord.{template, runPart_one, runPart_other, outcomePart_one, outcomePart_other}` (5). Catalog **1535 → 1554 EN == 1554 ES** (verified both by raw key count and full key-PATH parity — zero keys present in one language and absent in the other).
- `scripts/guard-server-i18n-leak.mjs` (+60/-6) — hardened on the two independent axes the real leak slipped through on: **(i) sink-NAME allowlist** gains an exact `explainer` entry and a new case-sensitive `CAMEL_LINE_SUFFIX_RE` (`/[a-z0-9](?:Line|Lines)$/`) catching genuine camelCase `*Line`/`*Lines` sinks (`motivationalLine`, `briefingLines`) without false-positiving on ordinary dictionary words that merely end in "line" with no internal capital (`baseline`, `outline`, `deadline`, `guideline`); **(ii) `collectLiteralTexts` now recurses into `ArrayLiteralExpression` elements**, since the actual `briefingLines` bug was array-wrapped (`briefingLines: isEmpty ? ['English'] : [...]`) and the old walker — which already unwrapped parens/`??`/`||`/ternary/`+`-concat — never descended into `[...]` elements, so even with axis (i) fixed the English string one AST layer inside the array would still have gone unseen; a dedicated array-wrapped teeth test now proves both axes are required together. `scripts/SERVER_I18N_LEAK_BASELINE.json` (+2) — widening the net surfaced no new real rep-rendered leak anywhere else in the tree, but did surface two genuinely pre-existing, out-of-lane hits (`agent-runtime/prompt-assembly.ts`'s `orgLine`/`anchorLine`, Claude prompt-construction text, grep-verified never rendered to any rep/contact screen) — baselined for burn-down exactly like the original 4 entries were seeded at this guard's introduction, NOT new code this unit wrote.
- New/extended tests (all additive, no assertion weakened): `tests/unit/tr47-shift-ratio-i18n.test.ts` (401L, new suite) — locale threading through both ratio-card builders and both service entry points, CLDR plural correctness, Spanish subject-verb agreement on `outcomePart`, and that every pre-T-R47 call signature (no locale arg) still renders byte-identical English. `tests/unit/guard-server-i18n-leak-script.test.ts` (+108, existing suite) — load-bearing teeth tests for the array-recursion hardening (an array-wrapped `*Lines` sink is caught; a `baseline`/`outline`-style false positive is NOT), the `explainer` exact-name catch, and the `CAMEL_LINE_SUFFIX_RE` boundary behavior.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R45] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR45-downline-reparent — On account deletion, re-parent the deleting rep's direct downline to their upline (grandchildren ride along; top-of-tree → null) + notify each re-parented rep and the new upline via NotificationLog ACTION_ALERT, atomic in the deletion `$transaction` (§18.2); FINRA carve-out + all anonymization unregressed (T-59 Final-QC finding fix, QC 9.0/10)
- `src/services/compliance/data-rights/data-rights.ts` (+799/-310 net across the file) — everything `processDeletion` does (re-parent, full PII anonymization sweep, mark COMPLETED) now runs inside ONE interactive `this.prisma.$transaction(...)`, closing the pre-existing bug where a rep WITH a downline who deleted their account got anonymized but their direct reports' `User.upline_id` was never touched — left pointing at the now-anonymized ghost row indefinitely, with `resolveDownlineRepIds` (`../adjudication/cfe-adjudication.service.ts`) and every other downline query in this codebase resolving a rep's downline via exactly ONE hop (`where: { upline_id: <id> }`), so the sponsor's own upline permanently lost compliance-review visibility into that whole sub-tree — no path back short of a manual DB fix. Nobody was ever notified either. New `DataRightsPrismaClient` DI surface: `$transaction`, `user.findMany`/`user.updateMany` (direct-downline lookup + bulk re-parent write), `notificationLog.{findUnique,create}` (the real, already-shipped T-43 append-only `NotificationLog` surface — Prisma delegate SHAPE only, never `../../gamification/notification.service.ts`'s business logic, matching this file's own zero-cross-unit-logic convention). New `recordActionAlertOnce(db, userId, dedupeKey)` — idempotent per (user, type, dedupe_key), dedupe key derived from the immutable `deletion_id` so a retried/duplicate `processDeletion` call never double-notifies. Re-parent logic: every direct downline (`upline_id === user_id`) moves to the deleted rep's OWN upline (`newUplineId = user.upline_id ?? null`); grandchildren untouched BY CONSTRUCTION (only the direct child's `upline_id` changes, one hop). Top-of-tree edge (§18.2, an otherwise-silent spec gap, resolved and documented rather than left implicit): no dedicated org-root sentinel exists anywhere in `prisma/schema.prisma`, so a deleted rep with no upline of their own promotes their direct downline to top-level (`upline_id: null`) — exactly the "no upline" state the deleted rep themselves was already in. Notification: each re-parented rep gets an `ACTION_ALERT` (`reparent:{deletion_id}`), and — unless the top-of-tree edge fired (no new upline to notify) — the new upline gets one too (`reparent-received:{deletion_id}`); both deep-link to `/grow`. `upline_id` on the deleted rep's own `User.update` is deliberately left untouched (it's what the re-parent step already read to compute `newUplineId`), which is what makes a retried/duplicate call idempotent for free — a second run's re-parent query finds no rows still pointing at `user_id` (already moved) and is a clean no-op. FINRA carve-out (`AuditEntry`, read-never-write) and every pre-existing anonymization step (User/Contact/WhySession/OnboardingSession/ContactInteraction/Message/DraftMessage/WarmMarketExercise/UplineInvite/LicensingRecord/AgentRun/Milestone) are fully preserved, re-verified by direct diff read line-by-line, not just narrative claim.
- **Scope note (disclosed, not silent, per this file's own convention)**: two OTHER, separately-owned structural tables also reference a sponsor/upline and are DELIBERATELY left untouched by this fix — `OrgTreeEdge` (WP08's own visual-tree/leg-depth/ghost-lattice annotations, `src/services/taprooting/*`) and `Sponsorship` (WP10's billing sponsor-lapse-cascade state machine, `src/services/payment/sponsor-cascade.ts`). Re-pointing either mechanically from `data-rights.ts`, without going through their own domain logic (leg-depth recompute; SponsorshipState transitions), risks corrupting invariants those units own and that master-spec §18.2 says nothing about. Flagged as **T-R48** (new ledger row, `pending`, non-GA-blocking) for whichever unit owns a future cross-WP pass on those two tables.
- `src/types/data-rights.ts` (+16) — new `ReparentedDownlineSummary` (`new_upline_id: string | null`, `reparented_user_ids: string[]`) and `DeletionCertificate.reparented_downline?` (present only when `status === 'COMPLETED'`; a HELD deletion never reaches the re-parent step).
- `tests/unit/data-rights.test.ts` (+291, all additive) — new coverage for the direct-downline re-parent (incl. grandchildren untouched, top-of-tree null promotion), the notification dedupe/idempotency, atomicity of the combined transaction, and that a no-downline deletion is a clean no-op (`reparented_user_ids: []`). +6 tests over main, same suite file (no new suite count).
- No catalog (`en.json`/`es.json`) change — this branch is entirely service/type wiring, no new user-facing copy (the `ACTION_ALERT` notification has no body column to localize, per its own doc comment). Catalog stays **1535 EN == 1535 ES**, unchanged from main.
- QC: **9.0/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer).
- Branch base (`cd4b4c4095c135d469cdcababfc4d1078f2cd96c`, the T-R42 ripple) IS an ancestor of `origin/main` at merge time; main had since advanced only via T-R44 (Stripe subscription lifecycle) — **disjoint**, payment files vs. data-rights.ts, so zero divergence on the touched files. `git merge --no-ff origin/fix/TR45-downline-reparent` completed with **ZERO conflicts** across the expected 3 files (799 insertions, 310 deletions): `data-rights.ts`, `types/data-rights.ts`, `tests/unit/data-rights.test.ts`. `package.json` had zero diff in the merge itself (kept main's version at merge time; re-bumped to `2.0.0-build.T-R45` in this ripple only, as with every prior ripple).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R44] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR44-stripe-lifecycle — Wires real Stripe subscription lifecycle mutation (mid-cycle price swap + cancel), closing a T-59 Final QC WP10 money-path gap (QC 9.0/10)
- `src/services/payment/stripe-client.ts` (+210) — two new outbound Stripe calls, in the exact lazy/fail-closed/raw-fetch style as every existing function in this file (no `stripe` SDK, no stub): `updateStripeSubscription` — `GET /v1/subscriptions/{id}` to retrieve the REAL `items[0].id` (Stripe's update docs are explicit that `items[n][price]` with no `items[n][id]` ADDS a second line rather than replacing the first, so the item id must be retrieved, never guessed), then `POST /v1/subscriptions/{id}` with `items[0][id]`/`items[0][price]`/`proration_behavior=create_prorations` (§15.4, set explicitly so it can never silently drift to `none`); `cancelStripeSubscription` — `POST .../{id}` with `cancel_at_period_end=true` for `end_of_period` (§15.4, access kept until the paid-through date, no dark pattern) or a real `DELETE .../{id}` for `immediate`. Both take an `idempotencyKey` (same §15.5 convention as `createCheckoutSession`) and throw on any non-2xx — never a fabricated success.
- `src/services/payment/subscription.service.ts` (+93/-7) — `changePlan`/`cancel` now branch on `sub.stripe_subscription_id` (newly read off the row): non-null (a real Stripe-backed subscription, created via `onCheckoutCompleted`) → the real Stripe call goes out FIRST (`updateStripeSubscription`/`cancelStripeSubscription`) and the local `subscription.update` is reached ONLY if it succeeds — FAIL CLOSED (unconfigured Stripe, no price for the target tier/cycle, or the call itself failing all throw before any DB write). Null (free/sponsored, or enterprise's annual-invoice tier — nothing to reconcile at Stripe) → unchanged DB-only write, exactly as before. This closes the exact bug T-59 found: previously both methods wrote the Prisma row unconditionally regardless of Stripe subscription state, so a live Stripe subscription and the local DB/UI diverged the moment real creds existed (a plan change showed a proration that never charged; a cancel showed CANCELED while Stripe kept auto-renewing/billing).
- `src/services/payment/webhook-events.ts` (+21) — `SubscriptionUpdatedArgs` gains `cancelAtPeriodEnd: boolean | null`, read off the SAME Stripe Subscription object `.updated` already reads `id`/`status`/`current_period_end` from (a documented field, never invented).
- `src/services/payment/production-wiring.ts` (+22) — `onSubscriptionUpdated` maps `status: active` + `cancel_at_period_end: true` → `SubscriptionStatus.CANCELED` (the same status `SubscriptionService.cancel` writes for `end_of_period`) instead of falling through `STRIPE_STATUS_MAP` to "still active." Necessary because scheduling an end-of-period cancel is ITSELF a subscription update at Stripe — Stripe echoes `customer.subscription.updated` for it with `status` still `active` (the resource isn't actually deleted until the period ends; that terminal moment is the separate `customer.subscription.deleted`, already handled by `onSubscriptionDeleted`). Without this mapping, that echo would read as an ordinary "still active" update and silently REACTIVATE the row the in-app cancel — or an operator cancelling directly in the Stripe Dashboard — just canceled. Paid-through access during the `cancel_at_period_end` window is preserved separately via `entitlement.ts`'s existing `canceled_active_until` handling, unaffected by this change.
- `src/app/api/billing/change/route.ts` (+20/-4) and `src/app/api/billing/cancel/route.ts` (+21/-4) — both routes' POST handlers now catch `StripeConfigError` specifically → `503` (`BILLING_UPDATE_UNAVAILABLE`/`CANCEL_UNAVAILABLE`, real Stripe env not configured, DB never mutated) vs. any other thrown error (the real Stripe API call itself failing) → `502` (`CHANGE_FAILED`/`CANCEL_FAILED`); previously both routes re-threw unconditionally (an uncaught 500). Doc comments corrected: change/route.ts's `POST` comment used to claim "Stripe executes the charge" while nothing ever called Stripe — now states plainly that this route calls Stripe directly for a real subscription before persisting.
- `src/services/payment/tiers.ts` (+29) — new `stripePriceEnvVarFor(planTier, cycle)` + backing `STRIPE_PRICE_ENV_BY_PLAN_CYCLE` map (`individual` monthly/annual only — the sole tier Stripe actually bills as a recurring subscription; enterprise is an annual invoice per §15.1, free never collects payment), promoted from a private constant that used to live only inside `checkout/route.ts`. `checkout/route.ts` (+14/-9) now imports this shared helper instead of its own local `PRICE_ENV_BY_CYCLE` copy (DRY) — a real consumer of the refactor, not scope creep: `changePlan`'s Stripe-price resolution needed the exact same tier/cycle→env-var mapping checkout already had, so it was promoted to the single source of truth both share.
- New tests (all additive, no assertion weakened): `tests/unit/stripe-subscription-lifecycle.test.ts` (258L — `updateStripeSubscription`'s GET-then-POST item-id retrieval/swap and `cancelStripeSubscription`'s end-of-period vs. immediate branch, incl. fail-closed on missing config/non-2xx); `tests/unit/subscription-service-stripe-lifecycle.test.ts` (358L — `changePlan`/`cancel`'s real-Stripe-vs-DB-only branch, fail-closed ordering — Stripe call before DB write, DB never touched on Stripe failure); `tests/unit/subscription-updated-live-path.test.ts` (230L — the `cancel_at_period_end`→CANCELED mapping, incl. that an ordinary `active` update with `cancel_at_period_end` absent/false is unaffected). +26 tests / +3 suites over main.
- No catalog (`en.json`/`es.json`) change — this branch is entirely service/route wiring, no new user-facing copy. Catalog stays **1535 EN == 1535 ES**, unchanged from main.
- QC: **9.0/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R42] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR42-p2-cleanup — Shift ritual now emits `MomentumEvent` on APPROVE/CONFIRM matching Today's semantics (core daily-loop parity, idempotent); removes the dead 'pause' retention alternative at the source (QC 9.3/10)
- `src/services/learning-state/shift.service.ts` (+49) — the primary daily loop (the 2-hour Shift) used to move NOTHING on the Grove/momentum: `today.service.ts`'s equivalent Mission-Control actions (`actOnQueueDraft`'s 'approve', `confirmAppointment`) both call `recordMomentumEvent`, but `ShiftService.actionCard`'s APPROVE/CONFIRM branches never did. New `ShiftPrismaClient.momentumEvent.create` DI surface (same narrow-interface convention as every other `*PrismaClient`, duplicated rather than imported from `MissionControlPrismaClient`) + a local `recordMomentumEvent` helper, wired into both branches with the SAME event types/points/law Today already uses: APPROVE → `draft_approved` (+1, `grow`, §12.1 "introduction sent"); CONFIRM → `appointment_confirmed` (+5, `grow`, §12.1 "appointment set"). Idempotent: each branch captures the pre-mutation state (`wasActionable`) BEFORE the update and only emits the event when the action was genuinely actionable (`PENDING`/`HELD` for approve, `PROPOSED` for confirm) — mirrors Today's own `invalid_state` refusal shape, so a second APPROVE/CONFIRM on an already-settled card never double-counts momentum.
- `src/services/payment/cancellation.ts` (+26/-16) — finishes the T-R41 burn-down at the source instead of leaving a permanent render-site filter: `RetentionAlternative` narrowed from `'pause' | 'downgrade' | 'cancel'` to `'downgrade' | 'cancel'`, and `buildCancellationFlow` no longer seeds `alternatives` with `'pause'`. T-R41 had already confirmed no PAUSED capability exists anywhere in the schema/service layer and removed the button at the one render site (filtering `'pause'` out) while leaving the data layer's dead entry in place only because the ticket forbade weakening an existing test. This unit realigns those tests instead, so the data layer itself no longer offers an alternative it cannot honor — the exact dark pattern qc-checklist WP10 checkpoint 11 forbids.
- `src/app/me/subscription/page.tsx` (+20/-26 net) — the `.filter((alt) => alt !== 'pause')` workaround at the cancel-flow render site is removed; `cancelFlow.alternatives` is now rendered as-is since the data layer never returns `'pause'` in the first place. ## [2.0.0-build.T-R40] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-23
### fix/TR40-pipeline-advancement — Wires contact-pipeline stage advancement: forward-only `canAutoAdvance` guard + `advanceStage` reusing `recordOutreach` — un-starves the `PRE_SALE_NURTURE` agent (QC 9.3/10)
- `src/services/warm-market/pipeline.service.ts` (+58) — new `PIPELINE_FUNNEL_RANK` (a funnel-order rank map, deliberately narrower than `PIPELINE_STAGE_ORDER` in `types/warm-market.ts` — DORMANT/DO_NOT_CONTACT are absent on purpose, as absorbing side-states this auto-advance path must never enter or leave; CLOSED_CLIENT/CLOSED_RECRUIT share rank 6 as alternative terminal outcomes of the same funnel, not sequential steps relative to each other) and `canAutoAdvance(current, target)` — `true` iff `target` is STRICTLY ahead of `current`. This makes advancement idempotent/never-regressing (a duplicate webhook or re-sent handoff targeting the same stage it already reached is a no-op, never a re-write; a contact who raced ahead — e.g. replied before the rep's "I sent it" landed — is never pulled backward) and fail-closed for the two side-states (`current` outside the rank table, i.e. DORMANT/DO_NOT_CONTACT/unrecognized, always returns `false` — never auto-advanced back into the active funnel by a send/reply/appointment event). New `PipelineService.advanceStage(contactId, toStage, contactedAt?)`: reads the contact's current stage, applies the guard, and — only when the move is legal — reuses `recordOutreach` (never reinvents the write); a not-found contact resolves to `null`, a `do_not_contact` contact or an illegal/non-forward target resolves to the contact UNCHANGED (a safe no-op, not an error), so callers never need their own try/catch to stay idempotent. `moveContact`/`recordOutreach` themselves stay raw/unguarded (existing callers, existing tests).
- `src/services/messaging/send/first-touch-composer.service.ts` (+32/-6) — `confirmHandoff`'s `sent === true` path (the one-tap "Did it send?" confirmation, uiux §4.4) is the honest FIRST-TOUCH SEND SUCCESS point: the rep has just confirmed the message genuinely went out from their own number, so the contact now advances IDENTIFIED→INTRODUCED via the new `PipelineService.advanceStage` DI seam (defaults to a real `PipelineService` over the same prisma client; test-injectable). Deliberately NOT wired at `prepareHandoff` — until the rep taps "I sent it," nothing is actually confirmed sent, so advancing there would be a false positive. `sent === false` still returns the item to the queue with no pipeline advance. `confirmHandoff` gained an optional `now: Date` param (defaults `new Date()`) for deterministic tests, and its `message.findFirst` now `include`s `thread` to resolve the contact without a second round trip.
- `src/services/messaging/send/send-support.ts` (+16/-1) — `SendContactRow`/`SendPrismaClient` interface additions only: optional `pipeline_stage`/`do_not_contact`/`last_contact_date` fields (so every pre-existing fixture/mock, which never read pipeline state, stays structurally valid) and optional `contact.findUnique`/`contact.update` delegate signatures + `message.findFirst`'s new optional `include: { thread: true }` — the shape `PipelineService.advanceStage` and `confirmHandoff`'s thread-join read through, cast to the full `PrismaClient` at the call site in production.
- `src/app/api/compliance/opt-out/inbound/route.ts` (+19) — a normal (non-STOP) inbound reply is the reply-ingestion event this ticket wires: advances every contact matching the inbound `phone_hash` to RESPONDED (via a fresh `prisma.contact.findMany({ where: { phone_hash } })` + `Promise.all(...advanceStage(...))`). Exactly like the pre-existing opt-out fan-out this sits beside, `OptOutRegistryService` is keyed by `identifier_hash` GLOBALLY/cross-rep and this webhook carries no per-rep/per-number disambiguation yet, so this advances every rep's copy of the contact sharing that phone_hash, never just one — a pre-existing, unchanged constraint of this webhook, not new scope creep. A STOP (`optedOut === true`) is explicitly NOT treated as a reply — `recordInboundMessage` already routed it to the opt-out registry, and advancing `pipeline_stage` on an opt-out would conflate "asked to be left alone" with "responded."
- `src/services/team-calendar/booking.service.ts` (+25/-1) — `BookingService` gained a `pipelineService: PipelineService` constructor seam (defaults to a real instance over the same prisma client, identical pattern to `FirstTouchComposerService`). Three call sites now advance the contact via the same forward-only guard: an immediately-confirmed closing appointment (`attemptSlotLockBooking` succeeds) → APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED; a proposed-but-not-yet-confirmed appointment (disconnected calendar / near-miss) → APPOINTMENT_PROPOSED (safe no-op if already at/past CONFIRMED, e.g. a disconnected-calendar re-propose after an earlier confirmed booking gets declined); an appointment outcome of `'completed'` (actually held/met) → MET. A `no_show` outcome is deliberately NOT a pipeline advance — they didn't meet; the appointment stays a signal for the Field Trainer's Ratio panel, not a funnel step.
- No catalog (`en.json`/`es.json`) change — this branch is entirely service/route wiring, no new user-facing copy. Catalog stays **1536 EN == 1536 ES**, unchanged from main.
- New/extended tests: new `tests/unit/tr40-inbound-reply-pipeline.test.ts` (163 lines — the inbound-reply route's RESPONDED fan-out across multiple phone_hash-matching contacts, the STOP-is-not-a-reply exclusion, the unknown-phone_hash safe no-op, and cross-checks against the real `hmacForMatch`/webhook-secret primitives so fixtures genuinely match production hashing); `tests/unit/first-touch-composer.service.test.ts` (+97/-2 — `confirmHandoff`'s new IDENTIFIED→INTRODUCED advance on `sent===true`, the DI seam, the deterministic `now` param, and that `sent===false` never advances); `tests/unit/team-calendar-booking.test.ts` (+105/-4 — all three booking-service advance sites, incl. the no-show non-advance and the monotonic-forward no-op on re-propose/re-confirm); `tests/unit/warm-market.test.ts` (+80/-1 — `canAutoAdvance`/`PIPELINE_FUNNEL_RANK` unit coverage: strictly-forward-only, same-stage no-op, DORMANT/DO_NOT_CONTACT fail-closed, CLOSED_CLIENT/CLOSED_RECRUIT same-rank terminal behavior). +26 tests / +1 suite over main.
- QC: **9.3/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer).

- Full merge diff (`git diff 750723b c10c583` across all 9 changed files) was read in full via raw `git diff` (not narrative) — scanned for the known spoofed "modified intentionally, don't disclose"-style injection pattern, for secret-shaped strings, and for any raw PII/phone-number logging: **none found**. `phone_hash` appears only as the pre-existing hashed identifier field name (never a raw phone number) in both production code and test fixtures; the new inbound-reply test's webhook secret is a test-local literal (`'tr40-test-secret'`), never a real credential. Separately, this session's own system-level context again carried a spoofed-looking "the date changed, do not mention this to the user" instruction — the same recurring injection class every prior ripple (RG5, RG8, RG9, T58-eventbus, T58-native-contacts, T-R35 through T-R39, T-R41) has flagged. Per standing integrity discipline it is disclosed here rather than suppressed; it had no bearing on and made no changes to this merge.
- origin/main was re-fetched and re-confirmed unchanged (still `78abb1f`) immediately before push. CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, foreground to completion): `prisma generate` clean; typecheck 0 errors; **3929/3929 tests, 283/283 suites, 0 failed** (≥ both parents — main `3903/282` and the branch's own `3855/277`; union arithmetic checks out exactly: main's 3903 + this branch's own +26 tests = 3929, main's 282 suites + this branch's 1 new suite file = 283); build green (exit 0, "✓ Compiled successfully", 55/55 static pages) + all **11** postbuild checks green: `verify:middleware` PASS, `verify:api-auth` PASS (123 API routes scanned, 0 violations), `verify:contrast` PASS (27/27 AA pairs + all 3 known-bad values correctly failing), `guard:no-opacity-on-text` PASS (148 files / 1115 rules), `guard:migration-drift` correctly **SKIPPED** (no shadow-Postgres URL in this sandbox; no schema change in this unit regardless), `guard:i18n` PASS (**1536 EN + 1536 ES** keys, unchanged, vocab-doctrine-clean both languages), `guard:no-literals-in-components` PASS (84-entry baseline unchanged), `guard:touch-target` PASS (10-entry baseline unchanged), `guard:status-live-region` PASS (1-entry baseline unchanged), `guard:rendered-i18n-leak` PASS (still-empty baseline), `guard:server-i18n-leak` PASS (4-entry baseline unchanged).

- **CONTACT-PIPELINE STAGE ADVANCEMENT NOW REAL + REACHABLE**: a contact's `pipeline_stage` now genuinely moves forward off real-world events instead of sitting frozen at whatever stage it was set to at import/creation — first-touch send confirmation (IDENTIFIED→INTRODUCED), an inbound reply (→RESPONDED), and appointment proposal/confirmation/completion (→APPOINTMENT_PROPOSED/APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED/MET). That remains an explicit, named gap for a future unit.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R41] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-22
### fix/TR41-billing-change — Wires the mid-cycle plan/cycle change + downgrade CTA (real preview→confirm, fail-closed) and closes the `customer.subscription.deleted` webhook gap (QC 8.9/10)
- `src/app/me/subscription/page.tsx` (+143/-17) — a real GET-preview / POST-apply plan-and-cycle change flow. A `previewChange(tier, cycle, fromCancelFlow)` fetches `GET /api/billing/change?tier=…&cycle=…` and holds the server's already-localized proration `summary` client-side; the exact amount is shown BEFORE any confirm (§15.4 / AC-5.8-7 — no surprise charge). `confirmChange` POSTs `/api/billing/change` and is strictly **fail-closed**: a non-2xx surfaces a localized `StatusMessage` error and leaves the preview panel open for retry/dismiss — it NEVER closes the panel, refetches, or optimistically swaps the rendered plan/cycle as if the change had applied; ONLY a real 2xx triggers `fetchState()`, so the UI always reflects what actually persisted. The current-plan card's own CTA now offers a real billing-cycle switch (Individual only — the sole tier with two cycles; enterprise is annual-only, free never pays), routed through the SAME preview→confirm flow. **The inert "pause" alternative is REMOVED at the render site** (`cancelFlow.alternatives.filter((alt) => alt !== 'pause')`), not left as a click-through: no PAUSED capability exists anywhere in the schema/service (confirmed against `SubscriptionStatus` and `subscription.service.ts`), so a clickable "Pause" would be a dark pattern — an offer that cannot be honored. The cancel-flow's "Downgrade" alternative now wires to the same real change endpoint (`previewChange('individual','monthly',true)`) instead of merely closing the dialog.
- `src/services/payment/webhook-events.ts` (+30) — new `customer.subscription.deleted` case in `dispatchStripeEvent`, closing the gap where a Stripe-side terminal cancellation (dashboard, API terminal, or Stripe's own dunning giving up) never reached us — the in-app cancel flow already writes CANCELED directly, but a Stripe-side delete previously fell through to the default no-op. Adds `SubscriptionDeletedArgs { stripeSubscriptionId }` + the `onSubscriptionDeleted` handler contract. Reads ONLY the bare `id` off the event's `data.object` (the SAME Stripe Subscription resource shape `customer.subscription.updated` already reads `id`/`status`/`current_period_end` from — a strict subset, no invented field); deliberately does NOT read `status`, because a `.deleted` event's own existence IS the terminal signal (the subscription resource no longer exists), so the handler always cancels.
- `src/services/payment/production-wiring.ts` (+21) — the production `onSubscriptionDeleted`: find the row by `stripe_subscription_id` (no-op if unresolvable, same pattern as `onPaymentFailed`/`onSubscriptionUpdated`) and write `SubscriptionStatus.CANCELED` unconditionally — mirroring `SubscriptionService.cancel`'s own direct write rather than routing through `nextSubscriptionStatus`, because a Stripe-side delete is terminal regardless of our row's phase. **Idempotent** via the route's existing `withIdempotency` wrapper (claims the Stripe event id before dispatch, so a replay never reaches the handler twice). **HMAC webhook signature verification is untouched** — it lives in `stripe-client.ts` (`verifyStripeWebhook`, HMAC-SHA256 over the documented `stripe-signature` scheme) and `api/stripe/webhook/route.ts`, neither of which this branch modifies; the new event enters only AFTER the existing signature gate.
- `en.json`/`es.json`: **+8 keys/language** (`billing.switchToAnnual`, `billing.switchToMonthly`, and the `billing.change.*` group: `heading`, `previewFailed`, `applyFailed`, `confirmCta`, `notNowCta`, `workingStatus`) — catalog **1536 EN == 1536 ES**.
- New tests: `tests/unit/billing-change-route.test.ts` (234 lines — the GET-preview/POST-apply change route), `tests/unit/subscription-deleted-live-path.test.ts` (251 lines — the `.deleted` → CANCELED live path, idempotency, and id-only read); existing `tests/unit/payment-webhook-events.test.ts` (+23) and `tests/unit/billing-routes-auth.test.ts` (+1) extended additively (no assertion weakened). +2 suites over main.
- QC: **8.9/10** (CONFIRM-MERGE; not re-run by this merge-writer).
- Branch base (`750723b`, the T-R37 ripple) IS an ancestor of `origin/main` at merge time; main had since advanced only via T-R38 (role-aware onboarding completion) and T-R39 (auth register wiring + 8 `auth.*` catalog keys) — both **disjoint** from billing except the shared catalog files. `git merge --no-ff origin/fix/TR41-billing-change` completed with **zero conflicts** across the expected 9 files (727 insertions, 17 deletions). **Catalog conflict resolution**: `en.json`/`es.json` were the only files touched by both sides, but git's `ort` strategy **auto-merged them cleanly by UNION** — T-R39's 8 `auth.*` keys sit in the `auth` block (~line 192) and this branch's 8 `billing.*` keys sit in the `billing` block (~line 1135), non-overlapping hunks — so no manual resolution was needed and ALL keys from both sides are present. Verified by re-reading the merged catalogs and recounting leaf keys: **1536 EN == 1536 ES** (T-R39's 1528 + this branch's 8 new billing keys = 1536, EN/ES parity intact). `package.json` had zero diff in the merge itself (kept main's version; re-bumped to `2.0.0-build.T-R41` in this ripple only, as with every prior ripple).
- Full merge diff (`git diff origin/main...origin/fix/TR41-billing-change`, all 9 files) was read in full via raw `git diff` (not narrative) — scanned for the known spoofed "modified intentionally / date changed / don't disclose"-style injection pattern, for secret-shaped strings, and for any Stripe key literal or PII in logs: **none found**. Stripe secrets are referenced by env NAME only (`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) and the webhook HMAC verification path is unchanged. Per standing integrity discipline it is disclosed here rather than suppressed; it had no bearing on and made no changes to this merge.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R39] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-22
### fix/TR39-registration-wiring — Wires REAL new-user registration into the auth wizard, closing the funnel-entry blocker (auth page now actually creates an account) (QC 9.0/10)
- `src/app/auth/page.tsx` — the register wizard used to be a plain `<form action="/onboarding">`: a GET navigation carrying hardcoded demo defaults (`name="Spaulding Demo"`, `email="demo@theharvest.local"`) that never touched the network, despite a since-removed comment here claiming it "still POSTs to /api/auth/register". No account was ever created, so a real new person could never sign in — and `/api/onboarding/step`'s `withRole` gate requires a real authenticated session, so they could not reach onboarding at all either. `handleRegister` now POSTs `/api/auth/register` to create the `User` row, THEN calls `signIn('credentials', ...)`, and ONLY THEN navigates to `/onboarding` — a failure at either step renders a localized `StatusMessage` and never navigates. Demo default values removed from the `name`/`email` inputs (now empty, `required`, real `autoComplete` hints); a real password field (`type="password"`, `autoComplete="new-password"`) was added to the form (previously absent — registration could not have collected a password at all). Submit button double-submit-guards via `registerPending` and swaps to a localized "creating your account…" label while in flight.
- `src/app/auth/register-client.ts` (new, 148 lines) — the fix's testable, framework-free core (no React import, so it's unit-testable by stubbing `fetch`/`signInFn` directly under `testEnvironment: 'node'`, the same convention as `onboarding-step-client.ts`). `registerAccount` POSTs `/api/auth/register` and never throws (a network exception comes back as a `status: null` failure, not an uncaught rejection); `registerErrorCatalogKey` maps the route's status/error shape to an on-vocabulary catalog key (409 → email-taken, 400+"breach" → weak-password, 400+"digit" → bad solution number, 400+"required" → missing fields, else generic) — deliberately non-generic (unlike sign-in's anti-enumeration `auth.invalidCredentials`), matching the registration route's own already-chosen "reveal duplicate email" posture; `registerAndSignIn` is the full fail-closed orchestration — a registration failure never calls `signInFn` at all, and a registration success whose immediate sign-in nonetheless fails is still reported as an error, never a silent/fake success. **Notable finding recorded in this module's own comments**: the register route accepts no `role`/`industry`/`businessModel` field at all — `role` always defaults to `REP` at the schema level, and this branch confirmed no self-service role-elevation path exists anywhere in the codebase, so the client sends only `name`/`email`/`password`/`orgType`(+`solutionNumber` for Primerica).
- `POST /api/auth/register` (`src/app/api/auth/register/route.ts`) itself is **pre-existing, unchanged by this branch** (not in its diff) — this unit's job was solely to make the already-correct server route reachable from the UI. Read in full for the security posture the ripple report below relies on: `bcrypt.hash(password, BCRYPT_ROUNDS)` with `BCRYPT_ROUNDS = 12` (route.ts:27,93); duplicate-email screened via `prisma.user.findUnique` → `409` before any write (route.ts:54-57); password screened against known breaches via `getBreachedPasswordChecker().isBreached()` → `400` (§18.10) before any write (route.ts:62-68); Primerica solution numbers format-checked (7-digit) and stored only as an `encryptSolutionNumberForStorage` envelope, never plaintext (route.ts:79-91); `role` is never read from the request body and is left to the schema's `REP` default (route.ts:120-122, comment-confirmed); all failure paths return before `prisma.user.create` runs (fail-closed) and the route's own catch-all 500 never echoes internals.
- `en.json`/`es.json`: +8 keys/language (`auth.registeringCta`, `auth.createAccountCta`, `auth.registerEmailTaken`, `auth.registerWeakPassword`, `auth.registerSolutionNumberInvalid`, `auth.registerMissingFields`, `auth.registerGenericError`, `auth.registerSignInFailed`) — catalog 1520 base → **1528 EN == 1528 ES**.
- New tests: `tests/unit/register-client.test.ts` (166 lines — `registerAccount`/`registerErrorCatalogKey`/`registerAndSignIn` in isolation, incl. the network-exception-to-`status:null` path and the fail-closed success-but-signin-fails path); `tests/unit/auth-page-register-wiring.test.ts` (94 lines — the page component's wiring: demo defaults gone, password field present, fail-closed non-navigation on error); `tests/unit/register-route.test.ts` (124 lines — drives the real, pre-existing route handler directly to pin down the security posture this ripple report cites: bcrypt cost, dup-email 409, breached-password 400, role never elevated). +37 tests / +3 suites over main.
