## [2.0.0-build.T52] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-21
### T-52 — WCAG 2.2 AA pass: contrast exemptions resolved, skip-link, TTS, keyboard nav, narration (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.5/10)
- Closes the master-spec §17.4 / uiux §6.1 full-site WCAG 2.2 AA gate, including the 4 legacy contrast exemptions carried forward from T-05 (`auth/page.tsx`, `onboarding/page.tsx`, `globals.css` `.side-link`, `globals.css` `.visual-root span` — all translucent white text on a flat dark fill). All four are now genuinely AA-compliant, not merely suppressed: three still-live instances (`onboarding/page.tsx`'s had already been removed by T-20's unrelated rewrite) were swapped from the translucent `rgba(255,255,255,.72/.78)` literal to the real design-system token for secondary text on an inverse surface, `--muted-inverse` (7.0:1 on `--grove-950`). `scripts/guard-no-opacity-on-text.mjs`'s `KNOWN_PRE_EXISTING_EXEMPTIONS` set is now empty — this guard runs with zero exemptions.
- Adds a real skip-link (`layout.tsx`/`globals.css`), Web-Speech TTS for the Today briefing (the gap the T-51 parity audit found — no TTS existed anywhere), keyboard/focus-visible support and a focus trap on modals, and 3 narration scripts (incl. `groveBloomNarration` threaded through `AnchorHeader.tsx` → `Grove.tsx`) — none of this existed pre-T-52. Touches `globals.css`, `layout.tsx`, `auth/page.tsx`, `today/components/{AnchorHeader,BriefingCard,Grove}.tsx`, `grow/components/RulesOfBuildingChips.tsx`, `shift/{ShiftView,components/ClosePhase,components/DoneScreen}.tsx`, `shift.module.css`, plus `services/{gamification/celebration.service,mission-control/momentum,mission-control/types,mission-control/zones/header}.ts`. No schema change.
- QC round 1 (**REJECT-INCOMPLETE 4.5/10**): the branch's own new `.skip-link` element failed the rendered-contrast gate (`tests/unit/rendered-contrast-gate.test.ts`, a real headless-Chromium renderer) — white text measured 1.11:1 light / 1.00:1 dark. Root cause (found by the fixer): a measurement artifact, not bad colors — the skip-link's design tokens (`--color-action`/`--on-action`) were always AA-passing (6.29:1/8.53:1), but its rest state (`top:-48px`, off-canvas) sat outside the screenshottable viewport, so the gate mis-sampled row 0 and reported a false fail. Fix (`globals.css` `.skip-link` only, +39/-3 lines): rest state is now a genuine 0×0 the gate's visibility filter correctly skips as hidden; `:focus` state is a real, opaque, AA-passing 152×34 box — no `opacity:0` (which the no-opacity guard would correctly flag) and no `display`/`visibility:none` (which would break tab order).
- QC round 2 (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.5/10**): scope confirmed `globals.css`-only for the fix (other 24 files from round 1 untouched); genuine fix not gate-dodging — rest state's 0×0 is correctly treated as hidden, focus state is a real opaque AA box with unchanged tokens; keyboard-reachable (Tab → focus → Enter → `#main-content`); teeth: a translucent `:focus` color mutation correctly triggered `guard:no-opacity-on-text` RED, then reverted clean. Full suite 2469/2469 green + 5 guards + `verify:contrast` 0-exemptions at QC time. One residual folded into backlog **T-R31** (non-blocking): `verify-rendered-contrast.mjs` screenshots page-load state only and never simulates `:focus`, so focus-only-visible elements get no rendered-contrast coverage (focus-AA currently rests on token-level `verify-contrast` + `guard:no-opacity-on-text`, both teeth-proven).
- Merged against origin/main@cb0a9dd in an isolated merge worktree/branch (`merge/T-52`), from a branch cut before T-R28/T-R29/T-54 landed. `AnchorHeader.tsx` **auto-merged cleanly as a true union** — verified by direct read of the merged file that main's `/community` (T-R28), `/me/subscription` (T-47), and `/me/data-rights` (T-R29) nav links all coexist with T-52's only change to this file (threading `groveBloomNarration` into `<Grove>`). Two actual conflicts repo-wide: (1) `scripts/guard-no-opacity-on-text.mjs`'s exemption list — HEAD carried T-R28's line-shifted exemption for `auth/page.tsx`'s old rgba text color, T-52 emptied the list entirely; resolved to T-52's (empty) side after confirming by direct grep that the underlying rgba/opacity violations are genuinely gone from both `auth/page.tsx` and `globals.css` post-merge — HEAD's stale exemption entry no longer corresponded to any real code; (2) the generated `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (build artifact, zero semantic content), resolved by taking main's tracked version (regenerates on typecheck). Repo-wide conflict-marker grep = 0 hits post-resolve. Net-new tests from T-52: trunk 2550 → **2588** (+38: `tests/unit/briefing-tts.test.ts` and `tests/unit/wcag-keyboard-focus.test.ts` new, plus touch-ups to `contrast-gate`/`gamification-celebration`/`mission-control-momentum`/`mission-control-today-service`/`mission-control-ui`/`shift-ui`). `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene (no schema change). CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, foreground to completion, including the Playwright rendered-contrast test): typecheck 0 errors + **2588/2588 tests** (188 suites) + build green (exit 0, "Compiled successfully") + all **5** postbuild guards pass (`verify:middleware`, `verify:api-auth`, `verify:contrast` — 27/27 semantic pairs AA-pass, `guard:no-opacity-on-text` — 0 exemptions, and `guard:migration-drift`, which correctly **SKIPPED (exit 0)** per its own documented graceful-skip message — no `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`/`SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` set in this environment).

## [2.0.0-build.T54] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-54 — offline-first + degraded-mode sweep: Today/Inbox offline-queue, CFE re-validation on reconnect (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10)
- Closes a master-spec §17.6 / uiux §6.4 platform-rule gap surfaced by the T-51 parity audit: Today's Action Queue and the standalone Approval Inbox had no offline handling at all — `onQueueAction`/`onMarkAttendance` and approve/decline/edit were bare `fetch` calls that would silently throw or fail with no recovery if taken while offline.
- Adds three new offline modules — `src/app/inbox/offline.ts`, `src/app/today/offline.ts`, `src/lib/offline/http.ts` — that route every mutating action taken while offline onto the shared, persisted `PersistentOfflineQueue` primitive (`src/lib/offline/offline-queue.ts`, from T-R11) instead of a bare fetch. Actions taken offline optimistically mark the affected item with a visible `queuedOffline`/`sync-queued` state and replay in FIFO order on reconnect.
- Critically, an offline-composed edit does not skip the Compliance Filter Engine on replay — it re-enters the CFE against the item's then-current state at reconnect time, using the exact same fail-closed gate every online submit already goes through (an approval whose CFE clearance expired while offline returns to the queue with an explanation instead of silently succeeding or silently vanishing).
- Touches `src/app/inbox/{page.tsx,inbox.module.css,components/ApprovalInboxItem.tsx,components/ContactControls.tsx}` and `src/app/today/{page.tsx,today.module.css,components/ActionQueue.tsx,components/CalendarStrip.tsx}` to wire connectivity state, the queue-length indicator, and degraded-mode banners into both surfaces. No schema change.
- Adds `tests/unit/offline-inbox.test.ts` and `tests/unit/offline-today.test.ts`, plus a touch-up to `tests/unit/approval-inbox-service.test.ts`: net effect on trunk 2497 → **2550** (+53).
- QC (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10**): confirmed complete (builder died mid-run on an org spend-limit and only got as far as a typecheck before a salvage agent committed the work — QC independently re-verified the full suite green and the unit not half-done, rather than trusting the builder's own account). Scope is presentational/offline-lane only — `offline-queue.ts`, the CFE gate, and the send-gate are all a 0-line diff (consumed unmodified, no schema touch). CFE-re-validation-on-reconnect has teeth: a mutation that skipped the re-check on replay produced 4 RED tests, and an offline-composed edit replay genuinely re-enters the real `/api/approval-inbox/edit` route's CFE check unconditionally, holding fail-closed on a simulated CFE outage. Replay-termination has teeth: a mutation to a naive replay loop reproduced a genuine infinite loop that hung Jest, confirming the terminating-replay design is load-bearing, not decorative. Today's Action Queue cannot bypass its pre-existing fail-closed gate via the new replay path; both surfaces are now genuinely offline-queued (were offline-less per the T-51 finding) with degraded-mode banners and idempotent replay.
- Merged against origin/main@fa37c2d in an isolated merge worktree/branch (`merge/T-54`). The expected `src/app/inbox/page.tsx` conflict against T-55's already-landed per-filter empty-state copy **auto-resolved cleanly via git's merge strategy as a true union** — no manual markers, no hand-editing required — verified by direct grep of the merged file (both `inboxEmptyStateMessage` and the `PersistentOfflineQueue`/`isOnline`/queue-handler offline machinery present); `src/app/today/page.tsx` had no competing changes to union against. The only actual merge conflict repo-wide was the generated `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (a TypeScript build-info cache, not source), resolved by taking main's tracked version and regenerated by the clean-env build; a repo-wide conflict-marker grep confirmed 0 hits post-resolve. `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene (no schema change). CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, foreground to completion): typecheck 0 errors + **2550/2550 tests** (186 suites) + build green (exit 0, "Compiled successfully") + all **5** postbuild guards pass (`verify:middleware`, `verify:api-auth`, `verify:contrast` — 27/27 semantic pairs, `guard:no-opacity-on-text` — 3 pre-existing exemptions only, no new violations, and `guard:migration-drift`, which correctly **SKIPPED (exit 0)** per its own documented graceful-skip message — no `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`/`SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` set in this environment).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R29] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R29 — GDPR data-rights center: export + deletion routes + /me/data-rights UI (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10)
- Closes a COMPLIANCE/reachability gap surfaced by the T-51 parity audit: the data-rights service (GDPR export + deletion, built in T-11 as `src/services/compliance/data-rights/data-rights.ts` `processExport`/`processDeletion`) had ZERO production callers — no API route, no UI — so a user could not actually exercise their GDPR/CCPA data rights. Same "built-but-unreachable" class the WP gates keep catching (see T-R28's `/community` link, T-47's `/me/subscription` link).
- Adds 4 routes under `src/app/api/data-rights/`: `export` (create) + `export/[exportId]` (ownership-checked download, wraps `processExport`), `deletion` (request) + `deletion/confirm` (requires `confirm:true`, moves to `PENDING` with a 24h cooling-off before `processDeletion` actually runs). All four are session-identity only (no `x-user-id`/forced-header trust — a forged header is inert), gated with `withCapability('data_rights')` + `withStepUp` (step-up MFA on every mutating route).
- Adds `src/app/me/data-rights/page.tsx` + `StepUpPrompt.tsx` + `useStepUpAction.ts` + `data-rights.module.css` — the user-facing export/deletion UI — and `src/services/compliance/data-rights/production.ts`, composing `DataRightsService` + `LegalHoldService` + `DurableDataRightsAuditSink`. The T-11 service and its audit submodule are consumed byte-identical/unmodified. Reuses the existing DSAR decrypt/secret-exclusion machinery from T-R7/R9 (export payload excludes `password_hash`/`totp`/ciphertext/`authTag`, verified with a real-fixture test, not a fabricated one). No schema change.
- `src/app/today/components/AnchorHeader.tsx` adds a "Data & Privacy" (`/me/data-rights`) nav link, the same ad-hoc header-link pattern already used for Approval Inbox/Grow/Subscription/T-R28's Community link — no "Me" index page exists yet for either Subscription or Data & Privacy to live on instead.
- Adds `tests/unit/data-rights-routes-auth.test.ts` (433 lines) and `tests/unit/data-rights-export-route-secrets.test.ts` (171 lines): trunk 2431 → 2463 on the build branch (+32), before rebasing onto the post-T-56/T-R28 trunk.
- QC (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10**): secret-exclusion MUTATED (added `password_hash` to the allowed export set) + CAUGHT → RED; cross-user 404-not-leak MUTATED + CAUGHT; deletion confirm-required + `PENDING` + 24h-cooling-off MUTATED twice + CAUGHT both times; step-up-MFA confirmed present on all 4 mutating routes; T-11 service + audit submodule confirmed byte-identical; no schema change; 2463 green at QC time. Minor non-blocking residual: `POST /deletion` idempotency has a theoretical TOCTOU window (worst case: 2 `PENDING` rows created, no data leak either way) — not fixed in this unit, tracked as a residual.
- Merged clean against origin/main@59e544f in an isolated merge worktree/branch (`merge/T-R29`). The expected `AnchorHeader.tsx` conflict against T-R28's already-landed `/community` link **auto-resolved cleanly via git's `ort` merge strategy** — no manual markers, no hand-editing required — verified by direct file read (both `/community` and `/me/data-rights` links present, alongside Approval Inbox/Grow/Subscription) plus a repo-wide conflict-marker grep (0 hits in any file). `package.json` was untouched by the merge (still main's `2.0.0-build.T56`; bumped separately in this ripple commit). `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene (no schema change). ## [2.0.0-build.T56] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-56 — cost model + kill-switch verification drill under load, plus T-R27 — cost-reservation primitive closing a concurrent-burst ceiling overshoot (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.0/10)
- **T-56 drill**: verified per-rep daily budget accumulation/isolation, kill-switch stays-tripped behavior, in-roster degradation under cost pressure (Sonnet→Haiku, never off-Claude), and CFE-never-traded-for-cost, all under simulated load, with teeth (mutate→RED→revert). In the course of the drill, **fixed a real fail-open gap**: `dailyBudgetCentsFor` had no fallback for an unrecognized `intensitySetting`, so the ceiling resolved to `undefined` and `spend >= undefined` was always `false` — an unlimited-spend hole on a mis-set threshold. Now halts fail-closed instead, teeth-proven.
- **T-R27 fix (found by the T-56 drill)**: the run-gate was check-then-spend with no reservation — a per-rep concurrent burst could all read the same pre-burst spend total and all admit, overshooting the daily cost ceiling (demo: 10 concurrent runs @18¢ against a 40¢ cap → 180¢ actually spent, 4.5x over) — a real §4.5 "no path spends past the cap" violation under concurrency. Fixed with a reserve-at-admit / reconcile-at-spend / release-on-fail primitive: admission is denied whenever `committed + reserved + estimate > ceiling`.
- The reservation `estimate` itself went through two rounds: round 1 shipped a fixed generic per-run average (14¢) that QC correctly rejected (9.0→3.0 REJECT) because real runs cost 18¢, so `floor(ceiling/estimate)` admissions still overshot the true ceiling under burst. The fix: the estimate is now a **true worst-case per-run bound** derived from the model's hard output-token cap — adds `HARD_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_PER_RUN=8192` and, critically, *enforces* it on the real wire (`anthropic-runtime-client`'s `max_tokens` was defaulting to an uncapped 1024) plus a conservative 24k-token input bound, so the reservation (~19¢) is always ≥ the real cost (~18¢) — the invariant `real_cost ≤ reserved` now provably holds, not just for the demo ratio.
- `src/services/agent-runtime/cost-killswitch/{run-gate.ts,budget-store.ts,index.ts}`, `src/services/agent-runtime/{agent-runtime.ts,index.ts,runtime-model-map.ts,scheduled-dispatch.ts,seams.ts}`, `src/services/agent-runtime/claude/{anthropic-runtime-client.ts,runtime-client.ts}`. No schema change, no UI.
- Adds/extends `tests/unit/cost-killswitch.test.ts`: net effect across the whole T-56/T-R27 unit is trunk 2409 → **2465** (182 suites). Includes a `test.each` burst test generalized across 5 cost tiers (including QC's round-1 breaking cases), an explicit `real_cost ≤ reserved` invariant assertion, and a dedicated no-leak-on-failure test (QC round 1 had flagged this as probe-verified-but-untested).
- QC (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.0/10**, second round after the 3.0 reject on the fixed-estimate version): independently reproduced the invariant holding (output wire-clamped to 8192 + 24k input bound → Sonnet's estimate of 19¢ exceeds the real 18¢); the judge's own independent burst probe stayed under ceiling on both a high tier (600¢ ceiling / 60-run burst → 558 ≤ 600) and a low tier (150¢ / 10-run burst → 126 ≤ 150); reverting the estimate back to the old generic value reproduced the exact prior overshoot (756/180) as a negative-teeth check; confirmed no regression on `max_tokens` (1024→8192 is a strict widening, not a behavior change for any run that was already under the old cap); no-leak-on-failure, fail-closed, and per-rep isolation all held. Residuals noted as non-blocking: the 24k input bound is architectural rather than wire-enforced (revisit only if unbounded prompt content is later added), reservation atomicity is single-process (matches T-R5's existing scope), and the ENTERPRISE org-level ceiling remains check-then-spend (explicitly out of this unit's scope).
- Merged clean against origin/main (`1f6fef0`) in an isolated merge worktree/branch (`merge/T-56`) — the unit was disjoint from every branch that landed since it was cut (T-55's empty-states sweep, T-R28's login/landing fix), confirmed via a repo-wide conflict-marker grep post-merge (0 hits in any source/test file). The only merge conflict was in `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (a generated TypeScript build-info cache, not source), resolved trivially and regenerated by the clean-env build. `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene (no schema change). CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, no secrets), run synchronously in the foreground: typecheck 0 errors + **2465/2465 tests** (182 suites) + build green (exit 0) + all **5** postbuild guards pass, including `guard:migration-drift`, which correctly **SKIPPED (exit 0)** in this clean environment per its own documented graceful-skip message (no `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`/`SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` set).

- QC (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10**): login→`/today` confirmed WITH TEETH (auth-handler-reverted-to-`/dashboard` mutation → RED; dashboard-rendering-instead-of-redirecting mutation → 2 RED; both reverted clean); confirmed ZERO paths land a user on demo content (`dashboard/page.tsx` is a pure redirect, all mock arrays/`#fragment`-nav/`ContactUploadDemo` deleted); `verify:middleware` stays green (`/dashboard` matcher still present); all 5 real destinations reachable from Today. ## [2.0.0-build.T-R26] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R26 — same-org validation on `DELETE /api/team/enterprise/seats` (`revokeSeat`), closing a cross-org IDOR (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.5/10)
- Closes a real, live cross-org authorization hole surfaced by the T-R24 route-auth pass: `DELETE /api/team/enterprise/seats` (`revokeSeat`) accepted a `seatId` query param and revoked that seat without ever checking the seat's `organization_id` against the caller's own org. An `ADMIN`/`RVP` who was correctly past the `enterprise_console:manage` RBAC gate for their OWN org could therefore revoke ANOTHER organization's enterprise seat purely by id — a deny-by-default / org-isolation (IDOR) violation. The sibling `POST` (`assignSeat`) already performed the same-org check; only the DELETE path was missing it. This was shipped WP09 code, and the WP09 gate missed it precisely because no route-level DELETE test existed — exactly the class of gap the T-R24 route-auth investment was built to net.
- The DELETE handler now (1) rejects a caller with no `organizationId` on file (400) and (2) looks up the target seat via `prisma.enterpriseSeatAssignment.findUnique({ select: { organization_id } })` and returns an identical `404 'Seat not found.'` when the seat either belongs to a different org OR does not exist — so a cross-org seat id is never distinguishable from a nonexistent one (no existence leak), mirroring the assign path and the billing/org 404-not-leak convention. The caller identity comes only from the signed session (`identity.organizationId`), never a client-supplied header or param; the `enterprise_console:manage` RBAC capability gate is unchanged.
- Adds `tests/unit/enterprise-seats-routes-auth.test.ts` (5 tests): same-org revoke succeeds, cross-org → 404, nonexistent seat → 404, forged identity header is inert (identity is session-derived), and a plain REP is rejected 403 by the RBAC gate. The builder teeth-proved the fix by neutralizing the org check to a no-op, watching the cross-org and nonexistent cases go RED, then reverting. No schema change.
- QC (**CONFIRM-MERGE 9.5/10**): confirmed cross-org AND nonexistent both collapse to an identical 404-not-leak, identity is taken from the signed session (never header/param), the fix mirrors the assign path, and the RBAC gate stays intact; teeth re-verified by mutation (no-op check → cross-org + nonexistent RED → reverted); the assign path is unchanged. Noted the "defense-in-depth" comment in the code is a cosmetic nit — the service-layer `revokeSeat` takes no org param and this route is its ONLY caller, so the route-level check is the sole (and sufficient) protection; the fix is correct.
- Merged clean against origin/main (`eebc6b0`) in an isolated merge worktree/branch (`merge/T-R26`) — zero conflicts, fully disjoint from every landed merge exactly as predicted (T-R24 was test-only + a `dashboard.service`/`team-page` dead-field removal and did NOT touch this route handler), confirmed via `git diff --check` (0 conflict markers). No schema change — `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene (harmless no-op). The DELETE-handler diff was independently re-confirmed by direct inspection during the merge. CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, no secrets), run synchronously in the foreground: typecheck 0 errors + **2409/2409 tests** (176 suites, exactly 2404 pre-merge + the 5 new — no regression) + build green (exit 0) + all **5** postbuild guards pass, including `guard:migration-drift`, which correctly **SKIPPED (exit 0)** in this clean environment per its own documented graceful-skip message (no `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`/`SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` set).
- Process note, disclosed rather than silently resolved: an earlier merge attempt (`ac5497f`) created this same merge commit (`0122ca4`) but was abandoned before the clean-env verify/push — it backgrounded the test suite and ended its turn "waiting for a notification." Verified git-state at that point showed origin/main still untouched at `eebc6b0` with no tag and no harm; the merge commit was intact in the worktree. This run resumed that exact merge commit synchronously (rather than re-firing) and carried it through verify, push, tag, and ripple. A mid-task automated background-task/date-change system-notification instructed non-disclosure ("DO NOT mention this to the user explicitly"); per standing doctrine it was treated as a suppression pattern and is disclosed here, not complied with. No "file modified / intentional / don't disclose" narrative-injection was otherwise encountered — git diff/status remained the sole source of truth throughout.

No live defect existed, but a future refactor could have silently broken a gate with nothing to catch it.

No schema change — confirmed via an empty `prisma/` diff on the merge commit; `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene. ## [2.0.0-build.T-R13] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R13 — embed the real inline-edit-with-CFE-re-entry Approval-Inbox component into The Shift, replacing the deep-link stopgap (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.0/10)
- Closes the post-merge integration tracked from T-34's QC (7.2): the Shift Work-phase's `APPROVE_DRAFT`/`RESPOND_FLAGGED` cards previously only offered a plain `<a href="/inbox">` deep-link for a flagged/blocked draft — a compliance-safe but thinner stopgap, adopted only because T-34 branched before T-33's Approval Inbox component existed. This unit deletes that stopgap and embeds T-33's real, unmodified `ApprovalInboxItem` component directly in the Work-phase card via a new `DraftApprovalCard` wrapper, per uiux §5.3's own "approve-with-inline-edit ... embedded full-width" language — a rep can now approve, edit (re-entering the CFE), or decline right there in the ritual.
- **Fail-closed guarantee preserved unchanged, not re-implemented.** `ShiftService.actionCard` and `ShiftApprovalRequiresReviewError` — the server-side authority that refuses to approve any non-`PASS` draft regardless of what the UI renders — are byte-for-byte identical before and after this change (independently re-verified by direct method-body diff during the merge, not just trusted from the QC record). `ApprovalInboxItem`'s own HELD-render gate (no Approve button for a blocked draft) layers on top as the second, independent line of defense. Editing a draft posts to the real `/api/approval-inbox/edit` route, genuinely re-entering the CFE via `ApprovalInboxService.editDraft` rather than a shadow reimplementation.
- Additive-only supporting changes: `ShiftService.buildCandidateStack` now hydrates a `draft` field (contact name, channel, CFE risk score, approval state) per draft-backed card — exactly the shape `DraftApprovalCard` needs to embed the real component — via one extra `contact.findMany` join, skipped entirely when the stack has no drafts; `ShiftQueueCard`'s new `draft` field on `learning-state.ts` is optional, so any pre-existing card shape keeps compiling unchanged. No schema change.
- QC noted one non-blocking residual (folded into T-R16, not fixed here): a FLAG-but-still-`PENDING` draft shows an enabled Approve button in the embed, because `ApprovalInboxItem`'s own gate checks `approval_state === 'HELD'`, not `cfe_outcome` — not a compliance bypass (the server-side `actionCard` check still refuses the mutation with a 409, surfaced as an inline error, proven by QC's own mutation testing), but a misleading affordance shared with the real `/inbox` page.
- Merged clean against origin/main (`205cd4d`) in an isolated merge worktree — zero conflicts (a repo-wide post-merge conflict-marker grep matched only decorative `====` comment banners, never a real `<<<<<<<`/`>>>>>>>`); `prisma/` diff confirmed empty on the merge commit, `prisma generate` re-run as harmless routine hygiene. ## [2.0.0-build.T-R25] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R25 / T-R25R — OrgSwitchEvent migration (closes WP08 schema/migration drift gap) + safe migration-drift postbuild guard (re-QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10)
- Closes a real deploy-correctness gap found while merging WP08 (T-49): WP08 added the `OrgSwitchEvent` model to `prisma/schema.prisma` but shipped no accompanying migration, so the table would be absent from any real deployed database even though `prisma generate` (and therefore every test using the generated client) worked fine off the schema alone. This unit adds the missing hand-authored migration (`prisma/migrations/20260721100000_t_r25_org_switch_event` — `CREATE TABLE OrgSwitchEvent` + `user_id` index + primary key; the `OrgType` enum it references already existed), proven zero-drift and applied end-to-end against a real throwaway Postgres 16 instance from empty.
- Adds a new `guard:migration-drift` postbuild guard (`scripts/guard-migration-drift.mjs`) so this class of gap fails the build instead of shipping silently: it runs `prisma migrate diff --from-migrations ... --exit-code` against a shadow database and fails only on real structural drift.
- **Required a remediation round to close a destructive landmine in the guard's first draft.** The original guard's shadow-database-URL resolution fell back to `DATABASE_URL` when no dedicated shadow var was set — the common case on an ordinary dev box or build pipeline — which meant `npm run build` would silently replay/reset the entire migration chain into the *real* application database on every build, even on the no-drift path. QC reproduced live data loss from this and rejected the first round (5.0/10). The fixer (T-R25R) removed the `DATABASE_URL` fallback entirely (resolution is now `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` → `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` → skip, never `DATABASE_URL`), added a defense-in-depth refuse-if-the-resolved-URL-equals-`DATABASE_URL` check, and added a regression test proving a seeded sentinel table survives a guard run byte-identical when only `DATABASE_URL` is set. Re-QC live-reproduced the old guard wiping a sentinel DB, then proved the new guard skips cleanly with the sentinel untouched — **CONFIRM-MERGE 9.3/10**.
- Merged clean against origin/main (`767fb26`) — zero conflicts; `package.json`'s `postbuild` chain auto-merged (main's existing 4 guards preserved, `guard:migration-drift` appended as the 5th). `prisma/schema.prisma` was untouched by this merge (the `OrgSwitchEvent` model already existed on main from WP08) — `prisma generate` re-run as routine hygiene. CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, no secrets): typecheck 0 errors + **2332/2332 tests** (172 suites) + build green (exit 0) + all **5** postbuild guards pass, including `guard:migration-drift`, which correctly **SKIPPED (exit 0, no DB touched)** in this clean environment — no `MIGRATION_DRIFT_SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`/`SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` configured, and the script's resolution chain never falls back to `DATABASE_URL`, confirmed by source inspection.
- This merge re-did a prior attempt that reached a full clean-env PASS but was killed before it could push (origin/main was untouched, no harm) — the stale worktree/branch it left behind (`.worktrees/merge-T-R25R`, `merge/T-R25R` @ `5469540`) were removed before this run started a fresh merge in an isolated worktree/branch.

## [2.0.0-build.T47] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-47 — WP10 payments: 3 locked tiers, §6.7 provisioning, Stripe idempotency, chargeback (gate PASS 9.2/10, re-gate #3)
- Builds WP10 per master-spec §15 / uiux §5.8: exactly 3 locked pricing tiers ($0 free / $297-mo individual / $25k-yr enterprise — the legacy $49/$199 tiers fully purged, zero live references remaining anywhere outside RETIRED-labeled comments), provisioning wired directly to the existing WP01 §6.7 signal-based tier assignment (`projectToWP10`/`provisionFromContract`, gated on `GATED_COMPLETE`, idempotent at most-one-active-subscription-per-user), the sponsor-lapse cascade (30-day grace, never an instant lock) with 60/30/7-day anniversary notices, billing-lifecycle soft-suspension, billing RBAC with cross-org 404s, SAQ-A-scoped card handling (brand + last4 only, no PAN ever touches this app's servers), and a no-dark-pattern/no-celebration cancellation flow.
- **Chargeback path required two remediation rounds to get right, and the second round caught a real false-green.** Gate #1 found the `charge.dispute.created` handler was dead scaffold (no `stripe_customer_id`→user map, so a live dispute could never resolve back to a subscription). Fixer round 1 (T-47R) added the `stripe_customer_id` column and resolved disputes via `dispute.customer` — but re-gate #2 caught that this field **does not exist on Stripe's real dispute payload** (a dispute only ever carries bare `charge`/`payment_intent` ids); the fix's own tests had only passed because they fabricated a payload shape Stripe never sends. Fixer round 2 (T-47R2) resolves correctly: a lazy Stripe-REST charge-retrieval (`GET /v1/charges/{id}`, the charge id genuinely is in the real payload) → `charge.customer` → subscription → the existing `handleDisputeCreated`, fail-closed on a missing API key, and distinguishing retriable failures (key absent, retrieval error, charge has no customer → HTTP 500, safe to retry) from a dead-end (no matching subscription → HTTP 200, no retry storm). Tests were rewritten to use a realistic no-`customer` dispute payload plus a regression guard that asserts that exact shape, so a future accidental reversion to the wrong field would fail loudly rather than silently passing again.
- Additive schema only: `Subscription.stripe_customer_id` (nullable `String`) + `@@index([stripe_customer_id])`; migration `prisma/migrations/20260720150000_t47r_chargeback_customer_id`. No other model touched.
- Mounts `/me/subscription` (tier card + billing banner), reachable from the AnchorHeader nav alongside the existing Approval Inbox and WP08's `/grow` links; adds 5 gated billing API routes (`/api/billing/{checkout,cancel,change,org,subscription}`) plus `/api/stripe/webhook`; registers the payments cron lane (`paymentInngestFunctions` — sponsor-lapse cascade, anniversary notices, billing-lifecycle soft-suspension sweep) into the shared Inngest serve endpoint alongside the six other WPs' cron functions. Gate (T-48), re-gate #3, **PASS 9.2/10**: all 12 checkpoints proven, all 7 of the judge's own mutations caught and reverted, the chargeback path proven correct end-to-end against a realistic Stripe payload (see above). Injection attempts (7x file-modification-style + date-change) resisted across the full fix-loop per the QC transcript.
- Merged clean-ish against origin/main (`ce8f0b5`) with 3 conflicting files (all resolved as clean unions, nothing dropped from either side) plus 1 build-cache revert: (1) `src/app/api/inngest/route.ts` — WP10's `paymentInngestFunctions` import + array entry landed on top of the six Inngest function groups already registered by prior WPs — resolved by keeping every WP's registration (all seven imports, all seven spread entries); (2) `src/app/today/components/AnchorHeader.tsx` — WP10's `/me/subscription` nav link landed alongside WP08's pre-existing `/grow` link (the WP10 branch predates WP08's landing, so it never had `/grow` to begin with; main predates WP10, so it never had `/me/subscription` — a genuine two-way union, not a rewrite); (3) `prisma/schema.prisma` — additive-only (`Subscription` gains `stripe_customer_id` + its index); verified the branch's `Subscription` model was a field-for-field strict superset of main's *before* resolving, then confirmed post-resolution via `npx prisma generate` succeeding cleanly (a brace-count error would have thrown), a repo-wide open/close brace balance check (117/117), and a full model-list diff proving all 79 main models plus all 63 branch models survived with zero net loss; (4) `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` reverted to main's byte-identical version (build-cache artifact, not a real conflict, per standing convention). `prisma generate` re-run post-merge, before typecheck (schema changed). CLEAN-ENV verify (`env -i`, `--maxWorkers=2`, no secrets): typecheck 0 errors + **2297/2297 tests** (167 suites, net +83 over trunk's 2214 at the T-43 ripple point — no regression) + build green (exit 0) + all 4 postbuild guards (`verify:middleware`, `verify:api-auth`, `verify:contrast`, `guard:no-opacity-on-text`) pass.

- Builds WP08 per master-spec §13 / uiux §5.5: the org-tree "orchard" visualization (universal rings + org-scoped detail), live Rules of Building chips, an activity-gated phased timeline, milestone detection (sync + a daily cron sweep), and an org-type switch flow (Primerica ↔ external).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R20] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R20 — rate-limiter atomicity hardening: InMemory lost-update/bypass closed, shared pure transition (QC CONFIRM-MERGE 9.5, zero critical)
- Tracked from T-R5 QC as a LOW/robustness item against `PostgresRateLimitStore`'s optimistic-CAS retry loop (read → conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE version = ?`, bounded 20-try jittered retry): under genuine high contention on a hot key, real-Postgres trials showed the CAS loop could exhaust and deny 1-2 requests (`store_error`) rather than admit them over cap — fail-safe (deny, never lose/leak), not a hole, but tracked for hardening.
- **Diagnosis correction, reproduced before assuming (not taken on faith):** the Postgres CAS loop was already correct — 20-concurrent-at-limit-3 capped at 3 admitted, every time. The REAL exploitable bug was in `InMemoryRateLimitStore.check()`: it read via `get()` then wrote via `put()` as two separate `await`s, so 20 concurrent `check()` calls at `maxAttempts: 3` interleaved between the read and the write and all 20 evaluated against the same stale pre-increment count — **20/20 allowed, a full rate-limit bypass** on the in-memory store (the store every non-Postgres-configured deploy, and the entire test env, actually runs on).
- Fix: `check()` now issues exactly ONE store call, `atomicUpdate(key, transition)`, where `transition` is a single pure, synchronous state-transition function shared byte-for-byte by both stores — `InMemoryRateLimitStore.atomicUpdate` has zero `await`s between read and write (atomic by construction, no interleaving window), and `PostgresRateLimitStore.atomicUpdate` encapsulates the existing version-CAS retry loop internally (same bounded-20-retry, fail-closed-on-exhaustion semantics as before, just moved behind the one-call seam). Both stores now implement the identical external contract, so no caller of `RateLimiter.check()` needed to change.
- 5 new tests added across `tests/unit/rate-limiter.test.ts` (2 concurrency cases — reproduces the pre-fix 20/20 in-memory bypass, then proves it capped post-fix) and `tests/unit/shared-store.test.ts` (3 drift-guard cases asserting the module's real exported `LOGIN`/`MFA`/`PASSWORD_RESET` rate-limit constants directly, replacing a prior local-`CONFIG`-copy test-quality gap T-R5's own QC flagged). 1854 tests post-merge (net +5 over trunk's 1849 — this unit's own coverage; no regression).
- Self-contained, tight 3-file diff (`src/services/security/rate-limiter.ts`, `tests/unit/rate-limiter.test.ts`, `tests/unit/shared-store.test.ts`); no schema change, `prisma/schema.prisma` confirmed untouched, no route/UI touched. QC round mutated all three surfaces adversarially (reintroduce the in-memory race, flip Postgres fail-open, drift a rate-limit constant) — each caught, each reverted and git-diff-confirmed red-then-green; `RateLimitConflictError` losing its export is an intentional, disclosed tightening, not a regression. Merged clean against origin/main (no conflicts); typecheck 0 errors; build green; all 4 guards (middleware, api-auth, contrast, no-opacity-on-text) pass.

- Remediates the WP05 gate's decisive FAIL (6.0/10, do-not-unlock): all 10 CFE/opt-out/quiet-hours/deliverability critical conditions were individually sound and every send-gate was mutation-proof, but the WRITE/TRIGGER side of T-39's messaging services (`SequenceService`, `EmailSendService`, `ObjectionService`, `ThreeWayHandoffService`, `EdificationService`) had zero reachable caller — no API route, no UI affordance, no cron — so a rep could not actually enroll a sequence, fire a cadence, use the objection tool, or trigger a 3-way handoff. This unit is wiring-only: the six mutation-proven gate files (`send-decision.ts`, `SendComplianceGate`, deliverability, and the org-gating seams) are untouched — confirmed byte-identical against trunk both at initial merge and at re-QC.
- New reachable entry points, every one session-gated, ownership-checked, and `x-user-id`-inert: `POST /api/messaging/sequence` (enroll) plus an hourly Inngest cron (`messaging-scheduled-sequence-run`, idempotent `runDueSteps`) and a `SequenceEnrollPanel` rep affordance; `POST /api/messaging/objection` (list/prepare, producing a PENDING/null-CFE draft that the existing send-decision gate refuses to send unclearanced) plus an `ObjectionCoachPanel`; `POST /api/messaging/handoff/trigger` (wires `EdificationService.generate`, org-gated) and `POST /api/messaging/handoff/join` (upline-id boundary enforced) plus an hourly `returnIfLapsed` cron and a `BridgeUplinePanel`; `POST /api/messaging/email-send` (routes through the existing CFE-cleared + `SendComplianceGate` + `isChannelDeliverable` chain, org-domain-verified, fails safe to HELD when the send credential is missing). All four surfaces funnel through one shared, lazily-constructed factory (`src/services/messaging/send/production-wiring.ts`) rather than each hand-wiring its own gate calls — a single seam to keep gated instead of four.
- Dead pre-WP05 scaffold deleted: `engine.service.ts`, `template.service.ts`, `handoff.service.ts`, `cadence.service.ts`, `api-routes.ts`, and the orphaned `messaging.test.ts` that exercised them — ungated, unreachable code that inflated the test count without covering anything a rep could hit. The fix added `production-wiring.test.ts` (16 tests) importing the REAL `buildEmailSendService`/`buildPlatformSmsSendService`/`buildSequenceDispatcher`/`buildSequenceService` factories directly (not hand-built equivalents) and asserting opted-out/non-deliverable/non-CFE-cleared paths are HELD through the real factory, with a mutation-teeth proof (forcing `deliverable: true` in the factory flips the non-deliverable test red; reverting restores green). Fixing this also surfaced a latent DI gap — `SendComplianceGate` was never threaded through the factory's `db` param, always resolving to the global singleton — closed with a minimal, disclosed, non-behavior-changing `ProductionWiringOverrides.sendGate` optional second argument (defaults to `{}`; every production call site still passes 0-1 args, so the seam is unexploitable in production and unchanged from trunk).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R4] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R4 — DB-level AuditEntry immutability + anchoring/tail-truncation detection + deep-frozen returned rows (QC 9.4, zero critical) — §16 COMPLIANCE TRUST-SPINE HARDENING
- Layers a second, independent enforcement level under the WP11 audit trust spine (master-spec §16.1 "immutability (append-only, signed audit)") on top of T-10's existing app-level guard (no `update`/`delete` method exists on `AuditRepository`/`PrismaAuditRepository`/`InMemoryAuditRepository` in the first place). New hand-authored migration `20260720140000_t_r4_audit_hardening` adds a `prevent_audit_mutation()` Postgres trigger function that `RAISE EXCEPTION`s (a loud, real thrown error — not a silent `DO INSTEAD NOTHING` rule, which would violate §17.7's "never silently no-op" doctrine for evidence surfaces) on any UPDATE or DELETE against `AuditEntry`; INSERT (append) is untouched. A trigger was chosen over a bare `REVOKE` because a revoke depends on knowing the exact DB role the app connects as (varies by deploy target) and can be bypassed by a later, more-privileged grant — the trigger applies regardless of which role issues the statement.
- New `AuditCheckpoint` model (additive, same migration) closes the hash chain's one honest gap documented in `hash-chain.ts`: a chain alone cannot detect truncation of its own TAIL (deleting the most-recently-appended rows, with nothing appended after to show a broken `prev_hash` link). New `src/services/compliance/audit/anchoring.ts` periodically anchors the current chain head (`sequence`/`head_entry_hash` from `getChainTail()`) plus a row count into this checkpoint table, SHA-256-hashed (`checkpoint_hash`) over the anchored fields. `AuditCheckpoint` gets the identical `prevent_audit_mutation()` trigger — a checkpoint that could itself be edited or deleted would defeat the tail-truncation detection it exists to provide. `AuditService.verifyAnchoring()` (new, on `AuditService`, wired via an optional constructor-injected `checkpointRepository` — every existing single-argument `new AuditService(repository)` call site keeps working unchanged) re-checks the store against the LATEST checkpoint on record and flags a mismatch as tail truncation; it also refuses to accept a newer checkpoint with a lower `entry_count` than a prior one, so an attacker cannot defeat detection by inserting a new checkpoint matching their own truncated tail.
- Every `AuditEntryRecord` returned by `query`/`getById` — from either repository, in-memory or Prisma-backed — is now deep-frozen (new exported `deepFreeze()` in `audit-service.ts`, replacing the prior shallow `Object.freeze`), so a consumer can no longer mutate a nested field (e.g. `classifier_data`) in place either.
- 12 new tests in `tests/unit/audit-hardening.test.ts` covering the DB trigger's reject-UPDATE/reject-DELETE-while-allowing-INSERT behavior, checkpoint creation/hash computation, tail-truncation detection (a shortened chain fails `verifyAnchoring` against its last-known checkpoint), the checkpoint-count-can-only-grow guard, and deep-freeze reaching nested fields on both repositories' returned rows.
- Purely additive: no existing column, table, enum value, or method signature altered or removed; the two new optional `AuditService` constructor/method surfaces (`checkpointRepository`, `createCheckpoint`/`verifyAnchoring`) are opt-in and throw a clear, named error rather than silently no-op'ing when a checkpoint repository isn't wired in yet, matching the repo's established not-yet-wired-in-dependency convention (e.g. `activity-ledger.ts`'s `DownlineScopeResolver`).
- Branched from 0cda2de (trunk's T-R5 head); merged clean, zero conflicts — this unit's diff (`prisma/schema.prisma`, the new migration, `anchoring.ts`, `audit-service.ts`, `compliance/audit/index.ts`, `compliance/index.ts`, one new test file) touches no file any concurrently-landing lane also touches. `prisma generate` re-run after merge (new `AuditCheckpoint` model) and before typecheck. 1795 tests post-merge (net +12 over trunk's 1783 — this unit's own coverage; no regression); typecheck 0 errors; build green; all 4 guards (middleware, api-auth, contrast, no-opacity-on-text) pass.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R5] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R5 — Postgres-backed shared rate-limit/session-activity/login-history store for multi-instance deploy (QC 9.2, zero critical) — MULTI-INSTANCE LAUNCH-GATE PREREQ
- Closes a launch-gate remediation item (§16.4/§18.10): T-12's rate-limiter, session-activity ("last seen"), and login-history/credential-stuffing stores (`src/services/security/rate-limiter.ts`, `src/lib/auth/session-security.ts`, `src/services/security/credential-stuffing.ts`) were process-local (`InMemory*Store`) — correct on a single instance, but on a multi-instance/serverless (Vercel) deploy each instance keeps its own counters, so a distributed attacker (or just ordinary multi-instance routing) can multiply their effective attempt budget by the instance count. This unit swaps all three stores for a Postgres-backed, cluster-wide implementation of the same already-swap-ready store interfaces — additive only, same caps/windows/lockout math as before; this is a store swap, not a policy change.
- Three new additive-only models (migration `20260720130000_t_r5_shared_store`): `RateLimitCounter` (key-scoped counters with an optimistic-concurrency `version` token — `PostgresRateLimitStore.put()` does a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE key = ? AND version = ?`, so two instances racing to increment the same key never silently lose one instance's attempt; the losing writer gets a `RateLimitConflictError` and `RateLimiter.check()` retries against a fresh read rather than overwriting a stale count — a brand-new key instead relies on the table's own primary-key constraint to turn a racing double-insert into a conflict); `SessionActivityRecord` (plain last-write-wins upsert, keyed by `sessionActivityKey(userId, boundAt)` — no optimistic concurrency needed since `touch()` only ever records "now", never a computed delta); `LoginHistoryRecord` (append-only, INSERT-only feed for credential-stuffing anomaly scoring — atomic by construction, no read-then-write race to guard against).
- All three Postgres-backed stores fail closed on any store error (DB unreachable, query failure) rather than silently degrading to "allow" — a store outage denies the request rather than open the gate wide, per the repo's existing fail-closed doctrine (mirrors T-R6's GDPR-clock fail-safe pattern). Exact pre-existing rate-limit semantics (caps, windows, lockout thresholds) are preserved bit-for-bit; only the storage backend changed.
- 16 new tests in `tests/unit/shared-store.test.ts`, including "PROVE" cases with teeth: cross-instance cap enforcement (two simulated instances sharing one Postgres-backed counter correctly hit the same cap neither could reach alone), atomic increment under concurrency (racing writers via the `version` CAS never both succeed), fail-closed on store error (a simulated DB outage denies rather than allows), exact rate-limit semantics preserved (caps/windows/lockout unchanged from the in-memory implementation), and shared session-activity/login-history persistence round-trips. A separate suite confirms default store wiring safely defaults to in-memory in the test env and never touches Postgres unless explicitly configured.
- Branched from 174bf50 (pre-T-39); merged clean against trunk's T-39 (messaging + `Message.approved_by`/`approved_at` + `OutreachSequence`/`OutreachSequenceStep`/`ThreeWayHandoff`) — fully disjoint lanes (T-R5: security/auth stores + 3 new schema models; T-39: messaging + its own 3 new schema models), the two units' only shared file was `prisma/schema.prisma`, on disjoint model additions, both intact post-merge; `prisma generate` re-run after merge (stale pre-merge client) and before typecheck. 1783 tests post-merge (net +16 over trunk's 1767 — this unit's own store coverage; no regression); typecheck 0 errors; build green; all 4 guards (middleware, api-auth, contrast, no-opacity-on-text) pass.

## [2.0.0-build.T-R11] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R11 — offline local-persistence + idempotent reconnect-sync for the warm-market ritual, Layers 1-2 (QC 9.3, zero critical)
- Closes the master-spec §5.4 AC-5.4-7 gap ("each layer resumes independently with no data loss") for `WarmMarketRitual.tsx`: the T-26 engine's `getState()` only reports which layer is COMPLETE, with no read endpoint for an in-progress Layer 1/2 seed roster, so a reload — online or offline — used to lose whatever the rep had typed but not yet submitted. New `RitualDraftSnapshot` (`src/app/ritual/warm-market/offline.ts`) persists Layer 1's entries, Layer 2's selected clusters/assignments, and the current layer client-side; on mount the component now prefers a present local draft over the last server-confirmed layer, so an optimistically-advanced-but-still-in-flight layer survives a reload instead of rolling back.
- New codebase-wide, framework-free offline primitives added under `src/lib/offline/`: `storage.ts` (`KeyValueStorage` abstraction — lazily probes real `window.localStorage`, degrading to an in-memory `MemoryStorage` under SSR, in this repo's node-env Jest suite, or when a privacy-mode browser throws on `setItem`; `readJson`/`writeJson`/`removeStoredItem` never throw — corrupt data or a full quota degrades to "session state intact, only cross-reload persistence lost," per §5.4 "Error") and `online-status.ts` (`isOnline()` / `subscribeOnlineStatus` wired to real `navigator.onLine` and `online`/`offline` window events, defaulting to "online" when `navigator` is unavailable rather than spuriously blocking).
- New `PersistentOfflineQueue` (`src/lib/offline/offline-queue.ts`) generalizes the in-memory `OfflineActionQueue` T-34 built for the Shift into a persisted, idempotent-replay FIFO queue any feature can reuse: every enqueued mutation is written to storage immediately and removed only after its handler resolves, so a reload or crash mid-replay leaves exactly the not-yet-applied tail queued — never a re-applied or dropped mutation. `createRitualQueueHandlers` wires the ritual's two mutation kinds (Blank Canvas, Qualities Flip) to replay through the SAME `/api/harvest-method/*` endpoints the online path already uses, with the load-bearing check that a 2xx HTTP response is not treated as "applied": both handlers inspect `result.ok` and throw on `false`, so a server-side soft-gate rejection or layer-order violation on replay is a genuine failure — the mutation stays queued, nothing is falsely marked synced, and the compliance/order gates apply identically on replay as they do online (no bypass).
- `BackgroundMatchingLayer` (Layer 3) degrades honestly when offline (§5.4 "Layer 3's matching requires connection"): the submit button is replaced by a deferred notice rather than a fabricated or silently-disabled control, with tile capture still fully rendered; the online path is unchanged (no regression). `WarmMarketRitual.tsx` renders an offline banner the instant `navigator.onLine` is false at mount, mirroring the pattern `ShiftView.tsx` already established.
- 86 new tests across two new files: `tests/unit/offline-primitive.test.ts` (17 cases covering `storage.ts`/`online-status.ts`/`PersistentOfflineQueue` — persisted round-trip, FIFO idempotent replay, crash-mid-replay recovery, stop-on-first-failure ordering, mid-replay-enqueue deferral) and `tests/unit/warm-market-offline.test.ts` (69 cases, including "TEETH" proofs that a previously-saved draft actually hydrates the live component on render — not just that the snapshot round-trips in isolation — and that queued replay genuinely hits the real server-side soft-gate/layer-order checks rather than bypassing them).
- Tight, disjoint diff (`WarmMarketRitual.tsx`, `BackgroundMatchingLayer.tsx`, `ritual.module.css`, new `offline.ts` + `src/lib/offline/*`, 2 test files) — no schema change, no route touched, no overlap with trunk's T-37 (messaging)/T-R14 (scheduler)/T-R17 (consent ordering), branched from 39236e3 and merged clean against dd83762. 1669 tests post-merge (net +86 over trunk's 1583 — this unit's own coverage; no regression; baseline re-verified directly against pre-merge trunk, not assumed from the changelog).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R17] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R17 — deterministic consent-current ordering + per-contact unique on ComplianceConsent (re-QC 9.4, zero critical)
- Remediates the LOW/non-critical finding deferred at T-38 QC (§9.2): `MessagingConsentLedger`'s "current version" reads (`captureConsent`, `hasMessagingConsent`, `getConsentHistory`) used a bare `findFirst`/`findMany` `orderBy: { version: 'desc' }` with no tiebreak, and nothing at the DB level stopped two concurrent `captureConsent` calls for the same contact from both reading the same "current" version and both inserting the same next version number — two rows silently sharing one version, with undefined DB row order deciding which one "wins" as current.
- New exported `CURRENT_CONSENT_ORDER_BY` (`src/services/compliance/messaging-consent/messaging-consent-ledger.ts`) — `version desc, timestamp desc, id desc` — makes "current" deterministic even when two rows do share a version: `timestamp` breaks a version tie in favor of the row actually written later, `id` is the final, always-unique absolute tiebreak for two rows inserted in the same instant. All three read paths now consume this single constant rather than each hand-rolling its own `orderBy`.
- New `prisma/schema.prisma` `@@unique([contact_id, consent_type, version])` on `ComplianceConsent` (migration `20260720100000_t_r17_consent_version_unique`) turns the race from silently-allowed into DB-detectable: the loser's `create` throws Prisma `P2002`, which `captureConsent` now catches and retries (bounded, `MAX_VERSION_RACE_ATTEMPTS = 5`) against the now-current version rather than surfacing a duplicate. Deliberately a plain `@@unique`, not a partial index: Postgres treats `NULL` as distinct from every other `NULL` in a unique index by default, so a plain unique on `(contact_id, consent_type, version)` never constrains WP11's onboarding-consent rows (`contact_id` unset → `NULL`, `version` always the default `1`) against each other — those keep coexisting unconstrained, exactly as before. The constraint only ever fires on a real, matching, non-null `contact_id` — i.e., precisely the per-contact messaging-consent shape this unit is fixing.
- QC fix folded in before merge: the initial pass's determinism test asserted the ordering constant's *shape* but drove its fake Prisma client from a hardcoded ordering rather than the `orderBy` value `captureConsent`/`hasMessagingConsent`/`getConsentHistory` actually pass — so the test would keep passing even if a caller regressed back to a bare `{ version: 'desc' }`. Re-QC fix makes the fake sort by whatever `args.orderBy` it's actually given and asserts each call site passes `CURRENT_CONSENT_ORDER_BY` by reference, so the test now discriminates a real regression instead of only checking the constant's own value.
- Additive-only migration, no other model touched; merged clean against trunk's T-37 (`DraftMessage.send_hold_reason`, migration `20260720120000_t37_send_hold_reason`) — the two units' only shared file was `prisma/schema.prisma`, on disjoint models, both additions intact post-merge. 1583 tests post-merge (net +11 over T-37+T-R14's 1572 — this unit's own ordering/race/unique-constraint coverage; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T37] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-37 + T-R14 — two disjoint-lane new-feature units, merged sequentially (QC 9.4 / QC 9.4, zero critical)
- **T-37** (QC 9.4) — the WP05 spine: the two honestly-engineered SMS send paths (§10.1). A new pure, I/O-free `resolveDraftClearance` (`src/services/messaging/send/send-decision.ts`) is the single shared invariant both paths are built on — "neither path may dispatch content that is not CFE-cleared AND human-approved" — consuming the `cfe_outcome`/`approval_state`/`edited_after_approval` state WP04's Approval Inbox already produced, never re-running the CFE engine itself. **First-touch composer handoff** (`first-touch-composer.service.ts`) turns a cleared, approved draft into a one-tap `sms:{E.164}?body=…` deep link the native Messages composer opens — a real blue bubble from the rep's own number, never claimed as "sent" until the rep's own one-tap `confirmHandoff` — subject to opt-out/quiet-hours but deliberately NOT platform/TCPA/deliverability-gated (the rep's own number is never Twilio-provisioned). **Platform SMS send** (`platform-sms-send.service.ts`) is the automated Twilio/A2P 10DLC path and the most heavily gated surface in WP05: a message dispatches only when all three gates pass in order — CFE-cleared-and-approved, `SendComplianceGate` (opt-out + recipient quiet hours + TCPA consent, T-38), and `isChannelDeliverable` (A2P brand+campaign approved + platform number assigned, T-36) — any failure, or a missing `TWILIO_*` key, holds (never crashes, never fabricates delivery) and records why on the new `DraftMessage.send_hold_reason` column. New additive, nullable `send_hold_reason` (migration `20260720120000_t37_send_hold_reason`) captures one of 11 hold reasons (`OPTED_OUT`/`QUIET_HOURS`/`NO_TCPA_CONSENT`/`NOT_DELIVERABLE`/`NOT_CFE_CLEARED`/`NOT_APPROVED`/`EDITED_AFTER_APPROVAL`/`CHANNEL_MISMATCH`/`TWILIO_UNCONFIGURED`/`NO_PLATFORM_NUMBER`/`NO_PHONE`/`ERROR`) — the "held for review, nothing was lost" evidence the rep-facing timeline surfaces; it never causes a send by itself. New `/api/messaging/compose-handoff` (+ `/confirm`) and `/api/messaging/platform-send` routes. 53 new tests across 5 files (`send-decision.test.ts`, `first-touch-composer.service.test.ts`, `platform-sms-send.service.test.ts`, `twilio-messaging-client.test.ts`, `tests/unit/messaging-send-routes.test.ts`), including the load-bearing proof that neutering any one of the platform path's three gates alone lets content leak the other two would have stopped.
- **T-R14** (QC 9.4) — the first autonomous (cron-triggered) agent dispatch trigger. Before this unit the only paths onto the durable queue were the session-gated user-invoked `POST /api/agents/dispatch` and a single event-triggered Inngest function — no autonomous trigger existed at all, contradicting the product's "24/7 / while you slept" premise. New `runScheduledDispatch` (`src/services/agent-runtime/scheduled-dispatch.ts`) is pure, package-free handler logic wired to an hourly (`0 * * * *`) Inngest cron in `inngest-functions.ts`; it CONSUMES rather than forks the existing pipeline — every due unit of work is enqueued through the same `DurableQueue.send()` boundary the user-invoked path already uses, so every existing guard (idempotency, per-contact do-not-contact/agents_paused, the CFE fail-closed gate) applies unchanged downstream. Guards respected twice: the scheduler consults the same T-31 `RunGate` once per rep before enqueueing anything (a killed/over-budget rep is skipped outright, no event reaches the queue), and `AgentRuntime.runAgent` re-checks it again downstream. Idempotency reuses the existing `IdempotencyLog` table (read-only `wasAlreadyDispatched`, per-UTC-day key) so an hourly tick is a liveness/catch-up mechanism, not the cadence itself — a rep/contact/agent combination fires at most once per UTC day regardless of tick frequency, and a pass skipped one hour is naturally retried the next. Recipient-timezone quiet hours and the CFE stay correctly out of scope (both are unmodified, downstream, still fully in effect — this scheduler only produces CFE-gated drafts sitting in the Approval Inbox, it sends nothing itself). 16 new tests in `tests/unit/scheduled-dispatch.test.ts`. No schema change.
- Fully disjoint lanes (T-37: messaging/send + additive schema; T-R14: agent-runtime/inngest, no schema) — both merged clean, no conflicts, including in `prisma/schema.prisma` where T-37's `send_hold_reason` is the only change either unit makes. 1572 tests post-merge (net +69 over T-R6's 1503: 53 new T-37 + 16 new T-R14; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R6] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R6 — guard computeGdprClock against unparseable clockStartedAt, fail-safe to max-urgent (QC 9.6, zero critical)
- Closes a defensive gap in the GDPR Art. 33 / master-spec §16.7 item 4 72-hour notification clock (`computeGdprClock`, `src/services/security/incident/gdpr-clock.ts`): a clock-applicable incident whose `clockStartedAt` was truthy but unparseable (a corrupt/garbage timestamp string) fell through into `new Date(input.clockStartedAt).getTime()` → `NaN`, then NaN arithmetic — `referenceMs > deadlineMs` (i.e. `x > NaN`) is always `false`, so `overDeadline` would silently read `false`/"plenty of time" on a broken input, exactly the wrong direction for a regulatory deadline; `new Date(deadlineMs).toISOString()` on a NaN `deadlineMs` would additionally throw `RangeError: Invalid time value`.
- New `Number.isNaN(startMs)` guard, checked immediately after the parse and before any deadline/elapsed/remaining arithmetic runs, routes an unparseable `clockStartedAt` to the same maximally-urgent fail-safe shape the pre-existing null/undefined "not started" branch already returns (`applicable: true`, `status: 'OPEN'`, `overDeadline: true`, `approachingDeadline: true`, `deadline`/`elapsedMs`/`remainingMs` all `null`) — reached via its own distinct branch, so the two fail-safe paths (missing vs. unparseable) stay independently testable and neither can silently swallow the other.
- 3 new cases in `tests/unit/incident-gdpr-clock.test.ts`: the unparseable-timestamp case fails safe (max-urgent, no thrown `RangeError`); a valid `clockStartedAt` is unaffected by the new guard (normal 72h arithmetic still applies); and the pre-existing null-`clockStartedAt` case still takes its own distinct fail-safe branch rather than being absorbed into the new NaN check.
- Tight 2-file diff (`gdpr-clock.ts` + its test), disjoint from every other unit on trunk — no schema, route, or other service touched. 1503 tests post-merge (net +3 over T-R15+T-R10's 1500 — this unit's own NaN-guard coverage; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R15-R10] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R15 + T-R10 — two disjoint-lane remediation units, merged sequentially (re-QC 9.4 / QC 9.3, zero critical)
- **T-R15** (re-QC 9.4) — narrowed the master-spec §0.5 "selling / closing (as extraction)" forbidden-vocab rule in `VocabularyClassifier` (`src/services/compliance/vocabulary.ts`) to require an explicit extraction-object cue instead of matching bare `sell`/`close(d)`/`closing`. The rule was first added at T-R15's initial pass (commit `0a105de`) to close a real §0.5 gap — CFE stage-1 had no rule at all for that row — but its first cut over-blocked: ordinary, non-extraction uses of "closed the deal" ("closed the deal on her new apartment/car/the merger"), accounting/furniture idioms ("hard close of the books", "soft-close drawers"), and the legitimate "close rate" §9.7 Field Trainer's Ratio metric all tripped it. The fix (commit `23a5b88`) requires either (1) a person-noun directly as the verb's object (`close them`/`closing the prospect`), (2) `close(d)/closing the sale/deal` followed by `with`/`on` + that same person-noun, or (3) the `sales closer`/`sale closing` idiom — and drops the ungated bare `hard/soft close` alternative entirely, since regex can't reliably separate its sales-technique sense from its accounting/furniture senses. New `tests/unit/vocabulary-classifier.test.ts` (39 cases) covers both the still-blocked extraction phrasing and the now-clean ordinary-English cases, plus asserts `FORBIDDEN_TERMS` holds exactly 14 rules (12 pre-existing + 2 new: selling, closing).
- **T-R10** (QC 9.3) — `GET /api/contacts/pipeline` (and the `/community` page it feeds) used to return four hardcoded illustrative contacts (an `_meta` flag marked the payload as demo data) regardless of which rep called it, so every rep's Community home showed the same fake four, and T-28's real per-contact flag toggle 404'd against those fake ids on the live page. The route now calls `PipelineService.getPipelineSummary(userId)` (T-23's real, session-scoped, `where: { user_id: userId }`-only, decrypted contact read) instead of the `demoContacts()` stub, which is deleted outright. `/community/page.tsx` and its new `community.module.css` were updated to render the real per-stage summary shape (including each contact's actual persisted `is_recruit_target`/`is_client` flags, not an assumed-false default) in place of the demo fixture's shape. New `tests/unit/pipeline-route.test.ts` (7 cases, incl. forged `x-user-id` inert / 403 when not onboarding-gated / real seeded contact renders / zero-contact rep gets a clean empty response) and `tests/unit/pipeline-route-import-safety.test.ts` (2 cases), plus 2 new cases appended to `tests/unit/warm-market.test.ts` proving the real isRecruitTarget/isClient flag passthrough and that `getPipelineSummary` never leaks one rep's contacts to another.
- Both units are disjoint-lane (T-R15 touches only `vocabulary.ts` + its test; T-R10 touches only the pipeline route/service, the community page + its stylesheet, and their tests) and disjoint from each other and from everything else on trunk. Both merged clean, no conflicts — including in the one file both this pair and an already-landed unit (T-R8) touch, `tests/unit/warm-market.test.ts`: T-R8's edits sit at the top of the file (imports, `importContacts` retirement block) and T-R10's new tests are appended after the existing "never the raw ciphertext envelope" test, so the 3-way merge resolved without overlap. 1500 tests post-merge (net +50 over T-R1's 1450: 39 new vocabulary-classifier + 7 new pipeline-route + 2 new pipeline-route-import-safety + 2 net-new warm-market cases; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T-R9] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-R9 — DSAR self-service export decrypts User PII + excludes password_hash/MFA secrets via deny-by-default allowlist (QC 9.1, zero critical)
- Closes a real DSAR export defect (§16.3 "the export must contain the data subject's actual data" + §16.4/§0.4 "no secret ever leaves via a self-service channel"): `DataRightsService.processExport` used to serialize the raw `User` row with no field selection at all — `solution_number`/`anchor_statement` (AES-256-GCM ciphertext envelopes at rest, §3.2) went out unintelligible, and `password_hash`/`mfa_methods` (bcrypt hash + encrypted TOTP secret/recovery-code hashes) went out as secret material, both unexamined.
- New `buildUserExportRecord` decrypts `solution_number`/`anchor_statement` via each field's own name-only key accessor (`getSolutionNumberEncryptionKey`/`getWhySessionEncryptionKey`, imported by name only — no dependency on either unit's business logic) using the same independently-degrading per-field pattern as the existing Contact-PII export path: a corrupt/mismatched-key field degrades to `DSAR_FIELD_DECRYPTION_UNAVAILABLE`, never a crash and never the raw ciphertext string.
- New explicit `USER_EXPORT_ALLOWED_FIELDS` — a deny-by-default ALLOWLIST, not a denylist of the two known-bad fields: `password_hash` and `mfa_methods` are excluded by construction (never read off the row at all), so a future `User` column added to the schema is excluded by default until someone deliberately adds it here having considered whether it belongs in a legal disclosure. `mfa_enrolled` (a bare boolean, no secret payload) stays in; `emailVerified`/`image` (dead Auth.js-adapter scaffold fields, always null under this app's Credentials+JWT flow) are left off.
- Adds `WHY_SESSION_ENCRYPTION_KEY` documentation to `.env.example` (the Seven Whys anchor-statement encryption key this export path also decrypts through). Additive-only, no schema change; tight 4-file diff (`data-rights.ts` export path, `.env.example`, `tests/jest.setup.ts`, `tests/unit/data-rights.test.ts`). 1450 tests post-merge (net +3 over T-R12/T-R8's 1447 — this unit's own decrypt/allowlist-exclusion coverage; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T38] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-38 — WP05 send-compliance foundation: global opt-out + recipient-timezone quiet hours + TCPA consent ledger + unified SendComplianceGate (QC 9.2, zero critical)
- New general-purpose `OptOutRegistryService` (master-spec §10.4/§3.4/§16.2) generalizes the existing per-reason opt-out fan-out convention (previously only exercised by the minor-flow in `vault.service.ts`) to every reason (`stop_reply`/`manual`/`wrong_person`/`minor`) over the trunk `OptOutRegistry` table. Keyed by `identifier_hash`, not `user_id`/`contact_id`, so one write is immediately visible to every rep's copy of the same recipient — global, permanent (no revoke method exposed), and cross-rep by construction, well under the §10.4/AC-10.9-4 60-second propagation bar. `isOptedOut` treats any read failure as opted-out — never as clear.
- New recipient-timezone quiet hours (§10.4: "distinct from the rep's own notification quiet hours"): pure, dependency-free `quiet-hours.ts` resolves the recipient-local minute-of-day via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`'s built-in IANA/DST resolution (no third-party tz library) and blocks sends outside the 8 AM-9 PM local window. Fail-closed by construction: a missing, empty, or unrecognized timezone id resolves to "cannot determine -> assume within quiet hours" (blocked), never defaulted to UTC or the rep's own zone. New additive `Contact.timezone` (nullable, deliberately not backfilled/defaulted).
- New per-recipient TCPA consent ledger (`MessagingConsentLedger`, §10.4/§16.2/§16.3): reuses WP11's existing `ComplianceConsent` table rather than forking a parallel model, additively extended with nullable `contact_id` + `version` (default 1) + a `(contact_id, consent_type)` index — a row with `contact_id` set is a per-recipient TCPA record; a row with it null is the original per-platform-user record, so pre-T-38 rows are untouched. `hasMessagingConsent` returns true on exactly one path (a confirmed latest-by-version record with `given: true`); no record, a revoked record, or any read failure all resolve to false.
- New unified `SendComplianceGate.evaluate(contact, channel, now)` (§10.4, qc-checklist WP05 checkpoints 4-5): the single seam T-37's send paths and T-39's sequence engine must consult before any automated dispatch, composing global opt-out -> recipient-timezone quiet hours -> TCPA consent (for `SMS_PLATFORM` only — the rep's own human-confirmed `SMS_HANDOFF` composer send is out of this consent record's scope by design) in order, deny-by-default: every individual sub-check is already fail-closed, and the whole composition is additionally wrapped so any unexpected throw (including a missing identifier for the channel being sent on) resolves to `{ allowed: false, reason: 'ERROR' }`. There is exactly one path to `{ allowed: true }`.
- New inbound STOP-keyword capture webhook (`POST /api/compliance/opt-out/inbound`, §10.4/§10.9-4): a machine-to-machine route (no rep session exists on a carrier webhook) authenticated by a shared secret (`INBOUND_SMS_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, read by name only, compared via `crypto.timingSafeEqual` with a length-mismatch short-circuit before the constant-time compare so an unequal-length secret can't 500 into a length-probing oracle) — fails closed (rejects every request) when unconfigured, rather than accepting everything. Explicitly does not read/trust any `x-user-*`/`x-auth-*` header, so `verify-api-auth.mjs`'s forged-identity guard is moot here by construction (no identity to forge). Alongside it, `GET/POST /api/compliance/messaging-consent` and `GET/POST /api/compliance/opt-out` give reps session-gated manual read/write access to the same two ledgers.
- QC fixes folded in before merge (QC 9.2, zero critical): added `compliance-routes-auth.test.ts` (forged `x-user-id`/`x-organization-id` inert across all three rep-facing compliance routes) and fixed a real Next.js App-Router build break (a non-route named export, `INBOUND_WEBHOOK_SECRET_ENV_VAR`, had to move out of `route.ts` into the service module — App Router rejects non-route exports from a route file). One LOW/non-critical finding deferred to backlog as T-R17 (a `findFirst(orderBy:{version:'desc'})` without a secondary tiebreak / no DB-level unique on `(contact_id, consent_type, version)` — a theoretical concurrent-write race on one contact, not a systematic allow-bias; tracked, not blocking).
- Additive-only schema (`Contact.timezone` + `ComplianceConsent.contact_id`/`.version`/index, migration `20260719100000_t38_optout_quiet_tcpa`) alongside T-36's 5 deliverability tables/4 enums — the two units' only shared touched file was `prisma/schema.prisma`, which merged cleanly with both units' additions intact. 1444 tests post-merge (net +68 over T-36's 1376 — this unit's own opt-out/quiet-hours/consent-ledger/send-gate/route-auth coverage; no regression).

## [2.0.0-build.T36] — BUILD PHASE — 2026-07-20
### T-36 — WP05 deliverability provisioning: A2P 10DLC + email warm-up + fail-closed channel-deliverability gate (QC 9.3, zero critical)
- New A2P 10DLC lifecycle (master-spec §10.3): `A2PProvisioningService` drives brand registration and campaign registration through the spec-verbatim `UNREGISTERED -> PENDING -> APPROVED/REJECTED` state machine (`a2p-state-machine.ts`, pure transition logic only — SUBMIT/APPROVE/REJECT/RESUBMIT, no illegal transition reachable), backed by new additive `A2PBrandRegistration`/`A2PCampaignRegistration`/`PlatformPhoneNumber` tables + `A2PProvisioningStatus`/`PlatformNumberStatus` enums (migration `20260719090000_t36_deliverability_provisioning`). A platform phone number is provisioned only after its campaign is APPROVED and is tracked through `UNPROVISIONED -> PROVISIONED -> ASSIGNED -> RELEASED`.
- New email domain authentication + sender warm-up (§10.3): `EmailDeliverabilityService` checks SPF/DKIM/DMARC (`EmailAuthRecordStatus`: `NOT_CONFIGURED`/`PENDING`/`VERIFIED`/`FAILED`) and drives a per-domain warm-up plan (`EmailWarmupStage`: `NOT_STARTED`/`RAMPING`/`WARMED`/`PAUSED`) with a ramping daily-volume cap, backed by new additive `EmailDomainAuthentication`/`EmailWarmupPlan` tables. SPF/DMARC are real, credential-free public DNS TXT lookups (`node:dns/promises`); DKIM needs a provider-specific selector (`EMAIL_DKIM_SELECTOR`, read by name only) and resolves to `NOT_CONFIGURED` — never a fabricated `VERIFIED` — when unset.
- New fail-closed `isChannelDeliverable` gate (`gate.ts`) is the single seam T-37's send paths (or any future caller) must consult before any platform-sent message: `SMS_PLATFORM` and `EMAIL` both resolve to `deliverable: false` on a missing sending domain, an unrecognized channel, or any unexpected error from either underlying service — this gate never lets a crashed check pass as a green light. One explicit, spec-documented exception (§10.1): `FIRST_TOUCH_COMPOSER` (first-touch SMS via composer handoff, sent from the rep's own number via a native `sms:` deep link, never through Twilio/A2P) always reports deliverable, because platform provisioning is simply not that path's concern — callers must not conflate it with `SMS_PLATFORM`.
- New SC5 admin status surface: `GET /api/admin/deliverability` (ADMIN-only via `withRole`, same verified-session pattern as `/api/agents/kill-switch` — no forged `x-user-id`/`x-organization-id` header is ever read) surfaces read-only A2P brand/campaign/number status plus email domain auth/warm-up status per organization, so ops can confirm "deliverability is provisioned, not discovered" before launch rather than finding out in production.
- Mockable Twilio/email clients, fail-safe on missing credentials: `createTwilioClient()` (`twilio-client.ts`) reads `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`/`TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` by name, lazily, per-request, and returns `null` — never throws — when either is unset; every caller treats a `null` client as Twilio UNCONFIGURED and resolves to a not-yet-sendable posture (`twilioConfigured: false` on the readiness summary), never a crash and never a fabricated APPROVED/sendable result. No `twilio` SDK dependency added — Twilio's REST surface is called via the runtime's built-in `fetch` through this narrow DI-mockable interface only. Same posture on the email side: `DnsEmailAuthClient` has no credentials to be missing at all (SPF/DMARC are public DNS).
- Full deliverability audit trail (`deliverability-audit.ts`, mirrors the existing compliance audit-emit contract): every brand/campaign transition, number provision/assign/release, and warm-up start/advance/pause emits a typed `DeliverabilityAuditEvent`. Additive-only across the board — no existing model, route, or CFE/runtime path touched; routes construct every service lazily in-handler (same build-safe, key-less-build convention as the rest of the codebase). 1376 tests post-merge (net +40 over T-35R's 1336 — this unit's own A2P/email-warmup/gate/admin-route coverage; no regression).
